- **Bid-ask spread% and daily-MA trend context added to trade alerts, 2026-09-05 — informational only, both repos committed (`30ff939`/`81252ad`).** Follow-up to the yield-formula-gap discussion: Marcelo confirmed ranking stays yield-only, but wanted more decision-support data surfaced to Juan. New `AlertCandidate.bidAskSpreadPct` (`(ask-bid)/premium×100`) and `trendLabel` (`"uptrend"|"downtrend"|"mixed"|null` from spot vs daily MA25 vs MA99, reusing `technicalIndicators.ts`'s existing math via a new `fetchTickerTrendLabel`/`buildTrendLabel` in `generateTradeAlertCandidates.ts`) — neither field touches `rankCandidates`' sort. `refreshTradeAlert.ts` recomputes spread fresh, carries `trendLabel` forward unchanged (same treatment as `technicalNote`/`ivRank`). Frontend: new `Spread`/`Trend` columns on Trade Alerts' New Trade and Roll tables (desktop + mobile), plus a real pre-existing bug fixed along the way — the Roll Alerts mobile card's POP field had no null-guard (`formatPercentage(replacement.probabilityOfProfit)` called directly), unlike every other POP render site; fixed to match. **Verified**: both repos typecheck clean, backend unit tests pass 8/8, live in local dev (light+dark) — new columns render correctly, and pre-existing pending alerts (predating these fields, `undefined` not `null` in their stored JSON) confirmed to degrade to a clean "—" rather than breaking (`TrendBadge` guards both). **Not yet verified**: real non-null values against a live IBKR quote — attempted via a live per-ticker refresh, got a 502 before reaching this code at all, consistent with the well-documented weekend/no-market-hours option-greeks gap elsewhere in this doc, not a regression from this change. Confirm next time markets are open (a real "Run Alerts Now" scan or per-ticker refresh should populate both fields on fresh candidates) — also worth a `/ui-ux` pass on the new columns once real values are on screen to check truncation/wrapping at mobile widths with populated (not dash) content.

- **Order polling replaced with a push SSE notification stream, 2026-08-27** — (both apps): the order-status side of the toast system above originally client-polled `GET /orders/:id` every 2s, which only ever… *(full entry archived: PROGRESS-ARCHIVE.md)*
**Order-id collision bug found, fixed, deployed, and prod history repaired — 2026-08-27**: the new toast system surfaced two separate "SMCI — Open Order" toasts for what looked like one order — turned out to be two real `order_requests` rows sharing `ibkr_order_id = 5`, one a two-day-old row stuck non-terminal from a prior Gateway session, one today's real order. IBKR's `orderId` is only unique within one Gateway connection session and gets reused after every restart (the worker also restarted 5+ times today alone during testing, so ids 1-6 were each reused across 2-4 unrelated orders); `ibkrGatewayWorker.ts`'s fill/status listener matched purely on that number with no session-scoping, so a single real fill could flip every historical row sharing that id. Real position/P&L data was never at risk — `trades`/`positions` are keyed by IBKR's unique `ibkr_exec_id`/live holdings, not `order_requests.status` — but the audit-trail column itself was actively corrupting in prod through the whole testing session.

Fix, deployed to the VPS worker same day: `order_requests.ibkr_perm_id` (already in the schema since 2026-08-24, never wired up) is now captured on an order's first status callback and required to match on subsequent updates — IBKR's `permId` is globally unique forever, unlike `orderId`. A row already in a final status (`filled`/`cancelled`/`rejected`/`error`) is now excluded from ever being matched again, closing the gap where a terminal row's `ibkr_order_id` could still be hit by a future reuse. Added `reconcileStaleOrderRequests()` (`ibkrGatewayFetchOpenOrders.ts`), run at worker startup and on every reconnect: any local `submitted`/`partially_filled`/`cancel_requested` row IBKR's `reqAllOpenOrders()` no longer reports gets flagged `error` and its `ibkr_order_id` cleared before a reused id can hit it.

Prod history repair (one-off `tmp/` script, Marcelo ran it against prod `DATABASE_URL` — the harness's auto-mode classifier blocks Claude from running writes against prod directly): every corrupted row was reconciled against its actual matching trade by `(ibkr_order_id, same calendar day, same symbol)` instead of trusting its corrupted status. 11 rows had a real fill hidden behind a wrong `submitted`/`cancelled` status and were corrected to `filled` (including the SMCI order from the bug report, and one AAOI open that had been wrongly marked `cancelled` despite a real fill); 8 had no matching trade at all — including one, an AAOI CSP, that had falsely shown `filled` with zero supporting execution — and were marked `error`/unconfirmable with their source trade alert reverted to `pending`. 6 rows from today were left untouched as still genuinely in flight.

## Vision
Multi-strategy options trading system. Business user checks in daily, reviews suggested opportunities and open positions, approves/rejects/modifies trades. Trades execute (semi-automated) via IBKR once confirmed. Node.js + React + Postgres, hosted on Heroku.

## Status
Hosted on Heroku. Per CLAUDE.md (updated 2026-09-21), a push to `main` auto-deploys to the **staging** apps (`iorio-staging-*`) only; the original `iorio-reloaded-*` apps (`app.ioriore.com` / `api.ioriore.com`, future prod) have auto-deploy off and are promotion-only. **`iorio-reloaded-api` (backend) is on a Basic web dyno** (switched back 2026-08-25 after a one-day Eco experiment — Eco's 30-min idle-sleep was suspected of correlating with Scheduler one-off dynos getting SIGTERM'd mid-run; moderate confidence, not worth the ~$14/mo saved since this app runs all 7 Scheduler jobs). **`iorio-reloaded-app` (frontend) is on Eco** (runs no scheduler jobs, no exposure to that risk). Heroku Scheduler's per-job dyno size is set to Basic on all 7 entries (manual, dashboard only). IBKR paper Gateway live on a Hetzner VPS via a restricted SSH tunnel; `ibkrGatewayWorker.ts` also runs there as `iorio-worker.service` (systemd, `Restart=always`) — see "Order execution platform" below. **All 10 screens built** (see "Screen list"). 7 scheduled jobs on Heroku Scheduler (see that section). Telegram alerting live.

**Major architecture change, started 2026-08-24, mostly complete:** Iorio moved from a manually-entered position ledger to a real order-placing/monitoring platform where IBKR is the source of truth. Full detail in "Order execution platform" below — order placement, fills, closes, cancels, and rolls are all verified against real paper trades; a few edge paths remain untested (see that section's open items).

- **Stock P&L vs. covered-call cap — investigated and closed 2026-09-19, no change made (Marcelo's call).** Marcelo asked whether stock P&L on Positions and Ticker Detail respects the short call capping upside at the strike. Finding: the stock leg's unrealized P&L is uncapped (`(current − entry) × qty`, `routes/positions.ts` `/pnl` and its SSE stream), but the short call leg's loss offsets it exactly when the call is ITM, so **Total P&L is correct mark-to-market**; only the Stock/Premium split looks lopsided above the strike. Realized stock P&L is already right (called-away exit records the strike), and the payoff chart (`payoff.ts`) already caps max gain. Considered capping the stock line at the strike and shifting the call loss into Premium P&L (total unchanged); **Marcelo chose to leave as-is** since the split reflects each leg's own mark. Don't re-suggest unless the display confuses users.
- **Full IBKR API audit — findings 2026-09-14, not yet implemented.** Marcelo's standing highest-priority ask (2026-08-24), picked up now that real-time market data has been confirmed working account-wide (since 2026-08-31) and for BATS specifically (DRAM, confirmed today — see below). Focus per Marcelo's explicit instruction: find artificial fixed-second sleeps used in place of event-driven resolution, streaming subscriptions opened for a one-shot need (should be `snapshot: true`, or FROZEN for a closed market), and option/closed-market reads missing a FROZEN fallback. Full-codebase grep-and-read audit (not yet independently re-verified line-by-line by Marcelo). **Category 1 — fixed-delay waits, 2 found**: `fetchLivePrices.ts`'s `streamLivePrices` and `fetchLiveGreeks.ts`'s `streamLiveGreeks` both always sleep the full `frozenGraceMs` (3000ms) before emitting their first (FROZEN) update, even when every contract's data arrives in e.g. 500ms — unlike their own sibling one-shot functions in the same files (`requestLivePrices`/`requestLiveGreeks`), which correctly race a pending-count tracker against the timeout and resolve early. Everything else using `setTimeout` elsewhere in the IBKR code was checked and is already a correct event-driven-with-timeout-safety-net hybrid (races the real IBKR completion event — `tickSnapshotEnd`/`accountSummaryEnd`/`contractDetailsEnd`/`scannerDataEnd`/`openOrderEnd`/`execDetailsEnd`/etc. — against the timeout, not a blind sleep); `fetchFlexCashTransactions.ts`'s poll loop is legitimate Flex-report polling (IBKR's own documented "still generating" retry signal), not this anti-pattern. **Category 2 — streaming used for a one-shot need, 0 confirmed violations**: `fetchOptionChain.ts`'s `fetchQuotesForContracts` does use non-snapshot streaming mode even for one-shot callers, but that's a deliberate, already-documented and tested exception (snapshot mode measured worse for options specifically — ~54% vs ~83% price+delta completeness, see the 2026-09-09 entry above) — not an oversight. **Category 3 — missing FROZEN fallback, 3 clusters found**: (a) `fetchTickerQuoteSnapshot.ts` (backs Genosuke's Telegram `get_ticker_quote` tool, callable any hour) only tries REALTIME and gives up with `liveUnavailableReason` outside market hours instead of retrying FROZEN, despite this exact fallback already proven to work in `fetchLivePrices.ts`. (b) The whole one-shot option-quote path — `fetchOptionChain.ts`'s `fetchQuotesForContracts`/`quoteOptionChain` — has no FROZEN fallback at all for its non-`live` branch, which every one-shot option-quote caller routes through: `refreshTradeAlert.ts` (user-triggered, explicitly gives up with "try again during market hours"), `checkAssignmentRisk.ts`, `generateTradeAlertCandidates.ts`/`generateRollCandidates.ts` (via `evaluateRollCandidate`), and `runTradeAlertGeneration.ts`'s scheduled roll scan — all REALTIME-only with no retry, even though PROGRESS.md's own prior findings (2026-09-10) established FROZEN is *more* reliable than REALTIME for options generally, not just outside market hours. (c) The two nightly capture jobs, `captureMarketDataSnapshot.ts` (`run-daily-market-data-job.ts`) and `enrichScannerCandidates.ts` (`run-daily-screener-scan-job.ts`), run right around/after market close with REALTIME only and no FROZEN fallback for a quiet name's trailing tick. **Suggested fixes, not yet built**: (1) rework the two `frozenGraceMs` sleeps to race a pending-count tracker, mirroring the sibling one-shot functions already in the same files; (2) add a FROZEN-then-REALTIME (or REALTIME-with-FROZEN-retry-on-empty) path to `fetchQuotesForContracts`'s non-`live` branch, which would fix (a) and most of (b) and (c) in one place since they all route through it or a near-identical pattern. Needs Marcelo's sign-off before implementing, per the standing "ask before deciding" rule — no code changed yet.
- **Roll alerts + "sell call" for unstructured positions need to move into the instrument view — raised by Marcelo 2026-09-14, not designed yet.** Current roll-alert review lives on Positions/Ticker Detail's Position card (see 2026-09-10 entry above); opening a fresh covered call against an unstructured (bare stock) position has no dedicated flow today. Marcelo wants both processes reworked and integrated directly into the Ticker Detail ("instrument view") modal, since that's the natural place to originate an order — needs a design pass (what specifically changes vs. today's Position-card entry points, how "sell call" for an unstructured position gets triggered from the instrument view) before any implementation. Not scoped or built yet.
- **Trade Alerts "Run Now" getting permanently stuck as "already running" after a deploy — root-caused, fixed, and deployed 2026-09-11.** Marcelo reported the Trade Alert Scan SSE toast showing "Connection to the server was lost" and, after that, every subsequent "Run Now" click silently failing instantly instead of scanning. Root cause: a Heroku deploy sends `SIGTERM` to the web dyno mid-request; `runJob.ts`'s `job_runs` row (inserted `status: "running"` before the job body runs) was only ever updated to `failure`/`success` inside its own try/catch — a SIGTERM kills the process before that catch ever runs, abandoning the row in `running` state forever. Every later "Run Now" was then correctly-but-uselessly rejected by `runJob`'s own overlap guard (`JobAlreadyRunningError`, added 2026-08-31 for a real double-click bug), since it saw a "running" row that was actually dead. Confirmed live against prod via `heroku logs` (exact SIGTERM/H18 sequence lined up with the stuck row). **Fixed**: new `src/lib/installShutdownHandler.ts` — on `SIGTERM`, marks any `triggered_by: 'manual'` `running` job as `failure` before the process exits (scoped to manual only — scheduler jobs run in their own independent one-off dyno, unaffected by a web restart). Plus a 15-minute staleness backstop in `runJob.ts` itself for a hard crash that never gets a SIGTERM at all (OOM, SIGKILL) — a `running` row older than that (real runs take ~4-5 min) is treated as abandoned and superseded rather than blocking a new run. Both typecheck clean. **Unblocked prod immediately** via a manual `UPDATE job_runs SET status='failure' ... WHERE id=...` (run by Marcelo, per the classifier's standing block on prod DB writes) on the specific stuck row. **Pushed and deployed** (`10363af`, confirmed on `origin/main`).
- **Ticker Detail modal fully reloading (chart unmount, chain/technicals blanking) on a background alert refresh — root-caused and fixed 2026-09-11 (`iorio-reloaded-app`), deployed.** Marcelo reported the whole instrument view visibly flickering/reloading sometimes while just sitting open. Root cause: `TickerDetailModal.tsx`'s 60s background poll for pending-alert freshness bumps a `streamKey` counter to force the option chain to reconnect and recompute its must-include strikes (needed — `prepareOptionChainStrikes` only computes those once per connection open, see the 2026-09-01 SNDK note) — but the connection effect responded to *any* `streamKey` bump with a blanket reset of `overview`/`chartBars`/`technicals` too, not just the option chain. Since the candlestick chart (`TickerPriceChart`, `lightweight-charts`) is conditionally mounted on `chartBars` rather than just re-rendered, nulling it fully unmounted and rebuilt the chart from scratch — reading as the whole screen reloading, even though only the option chain's strikes actually needed refreshing. **Fixed**: added a `previousStreamSymbolRef` to distinguish an actual symbol change (switching tickers — still gets the full reset, alerts refetch, and position reload) from a `streamKey`-only bump (alert-freshness reconnect — now resets only `optionChain`/`optionChainError`/`streamError`; overview, chart, technicals, active expiry, and any in-progress order selection keep showing their last-known values across the reconnect). Verified live via Playwright against the local dev server: the option chain refreshes on its own brief spinner while payoff chart/Technicals/IV History stay stable, no console errors. `tsc -p tsconfig.app.json` clean. **Pushed and deployed** (`b26fc57`, confirmed on `origin/main`).
- **IBKR streaming connections pooling/capping — investigated and fixed 2026-09-11.** Follow-up to the 2026-09-11 finding that opening Ticker Detail on top of the streaming Positions table could stall one connection 35+s with no resolve/fail. Ran the dev server directly (own process, stdout visible) and reproduced live with Playwright + timestamped instrumentation at every stage (DB query, SSH tunnel, IBKR handshake). Findings: (1) the SSH tunnel is never the bottleneck — consistently ~3-4s even with 4-6 opening concurrently; (2) the 15s/18s timeouts fire exactly on schedule, so the timeout chain itself isn't broken — the original "35s+, never resolves" symptom is fully explained by the already-fixed `EventSource` auto-reconnect bug (two chained 15s timeout-and-silently-retry cycles looked like one long hang before that fix); (3) the real, reproducible contention is in IBKR Gateway's own connection-handshake acceptance (`ib.connect()` → `nextValidId`) — under a burst of simultaneous new-client connects, one can be frozen out for the full timeout while others succeed in 1-2s. **Fix**: new `src/ibkr/ibkrHandshakeQueue.ts` — a small FIFO concurrency limiter (2 slots) wrapping only the handshake step (not the SSH tunnel, which doesn't contend) in both `connectIbkr.ts`'s one-shot connections and `sharedReadConnection.ts`'s shared connection; each queued caller still gets its own full timeout budget starting only once it acquires a slot. **Separate bug found during the same investigation, more impactful**: the shared read connection's fixed `clientId: 43` could not complete its IBKR handshake *at all*, 100% reproducible, regardless of concurrency — isolated with a minimal standalone `@stoqey/ib` script (`tmp/`, deleted after) outside all app code: `ib.connect(43)` always timed out waiting for `nextValidId`, `ib.connect(44)` always succeeded in ~1.5s, and this survived a full `docker restart` of the Gateway container (done with Marcelo's approval) — meaning it's coming from Gateway's own persisted settings, not process/client state on our side. Fixed by switching `webReadClientId` to 44 (`sharedReadConnection.ts`) — verified live: shared connection now connects in ~5s total instead of timing out, and the original Positions+Ticker Detail repro now resolves in ~5s instead of 18-35s. Also found and cleaned up (with Marcelo's approval): two orphaned `tsx watch` dev-server processes from earlier sessions racing for port 3030 on every file save, and a stale MCP Playwright browser session — unrelated to the IBKR fix, just dev-environment hygiene. `tsc --noEmit` clean. **Pushed and deployed** (`813d369`, confirmed on `origin/main`). No VPS deploy needed — all three changed files (`connectIbkr.ts`, `sharedReadConnection.ts`, new `ibkrHandshakeQueue.ts`) run on the web dyno only, not `ibkrGatewayWorker.ts` or any other `ibkrGateway*`-prefixed file.
- **Restart-safety audit (deploy/crash mid-market-hours) — investigated, two real gaps found and fixed 2026-09-09, deployed.** Marcelo asked whether a Heroku deploy or worker restart during market hours could lose data/state. Full-repo investigation (see PROGRESS entries below for the durable architecture this confirmed: `order_requests`+NOTIFY, `reconcilePositionsFromIbkr` treating IBKR as source of truth, the 2026-09-08 worker-zombie hardening) found everything else already self-heals correctly, but two genuine gaps: (1) `POST /orders/:id/confirm` (`positions.ts`) committed its DB transaction and NOTIFY *before* sending the HTTP response — if the web dyno restarted in that narrow window, the order was already confirmed/on its way to IBKR but the client saw a failed request; retrying hit a 404 ("no pending order"), which could read as "never went through" and risk the user building a genuine duplicate order. (2) An opening execution with no `position_legs` row yet is buffered in-memory (`pendingOpeningExecutions`, `ibkrGatewayWorker.ts`) until the next 60s reconciliation pass creates the leg — a worker restart in that window loses the buffer; the position itself still self-heals via `reconcilePositionsFromIbkr`, but that one fill's row in the Trade Blotter could be permanently skipped. **Fixed**: (1) the confirm route no longer 404s on an already-processed order — it returns the current state instead, and the actual status flip is now an atomic `WHERE status = 'pending_confirmation'` conditional update (read back via `.returning`) so two near-simultaneous calls can't both fall through to the NOTIFY. (2) New `src/ibkr/ibkrGatewayReplayRecentExecutions.ts` — on every IBKR (re)connect, calls `reqExecutions` for the last 6 hours and replays them through the same already-idempotent `recordExecution` path live fills use (`trades.ibkr_exec_id` is UNIQUE, so reprocessing an already-recorded execution is a no-op); this recovers a buffered-then-lost fill from IBKR's own execution log instead of relying on the live event having survived. Neither change touches schema. **Tested**: (1) verified live against the local dev server/DB — 5 truly concurrent confirm calls on one fresh order all returned 200 with the exact same `updatedAt` (only one actually wrote), confirming the atomic update closes the race. (2) `ibkrGatewayReplayRecentExecutions.ts` unit-tested against a mocked IBKR API. **Deliberately not tested against the real VPS worker** — it's live right now with real paper positions on the fixed clientId 42; a second local connection on that same id risked kicking the live one off mid-market-hours. Confirming fix (2) against real IBKR is left to organic observation: watch for the `replayRecentIbkrExecutions: replayed N execution(s)` log line next time the worker restarts naturally. `tsc --noEmit` clean on both changes. Both changes deployed (`22382ff`): fix (1) via Heroku auto-deploy; fix (2) is live on the VPS worker (HEAD `33b9285` includes it, and the `replayRecentIbkrExecutions: replayed 0 execution(s)` log line appears on real worker restarts since 2026-09-10 — verified 2026-09-18). Only the zero-fill case has been observed so far; a replay that recovers a real lost fill hasn't happened yet.

- **Screener redesign — discovery tool + Shortlist tab, built 2026-09-05, deployed by 2026-09-09** (confirmed by the 2026-09-09/09-10 follow-up entries below, which describe this scan job and `ScreenerTab.tsx` running live in prod). The old "Screener" page was really just the shortlist of monitored tickers; it's now a real discovery tool. `ScreenerPage.tsx` has two tabs: **Screener** (new — filters against a daily IBKR market-scanner snapshot, "Add to Shortlist" per candidate) and **Shortlist** (today's exact existing behavior, moved unchanged). Full rename to match: the old `screener.ts`/`api/screener.ts` (shortlist CRUD) are now `shortlist.ts`/`api/shortlist.ts` mounted at `/shortlist`; the new discovery feature took over the `screener.ts`/`/screener` names, since that's what actually screens the market now. `shortlist_entries`/`tickers`/`market_data_snapshots` are unchanged — they already meant exactly "shortlist," no DB rename needed. New table `screener_scan_results` (independent of `tickers` — a scan candidate only gets a real `tickers` row once actually added to the shortlist, via the shared `findOrCreateTicker.ts` helper extracted from the old inline logic). New nightly job `job:daily-screener-scan` runs 3 IBKR scan codes (`HIGH_OPT_IMP_VOLAT_OVER_HIST` primary, `HOT_BY_OPT_VOLUME`, `HIGH_OPT_IMP_VOLAT`), pools/dedupes candidates, enriches each (price, avg share/option volume, call/put OI, bid/ask spread, IV) via sequential `reqMktData` calls on one shared connection, purges anything not refreshed that day, and upserts latest-per-symbol. `TickerDetailModal` gained its own "Add to Shortlist" button, gated on a new `isShortlisted` field added to the ticker overview SSE payload. Genosuke's shortlist tool names/routes renamed to match (`add_shortlist_ticker` etc.). Verified end-to-end live (not just typechecked): migration applied, both repos typecheck clean, existing test suite passes (36/36), full flow driven live in a browser (add-to-shortlist, Add-button hide/show, mobile viewport), test data cleaned up. **Not yet verified**: the scanner's `benchmark`/`projection` field parsing (feeds `iv_vs_hist_ratio`) — markets were closed (Saturday) when this was built, so `tmp/testScannerSubscription.ts` returned zero rows for every scan code ("no items retrieved") and the actual field mapping is unconfirmed (IBKR's own TWS API docs don't describe what these fields contain, confirmed 2026-09-05). The job code parses defensively (falls back to `null` rather than a wrong number if the format doesn't match); re-run that script during US market hours before trusting `iv_vs_hist_ratio`, and adjust `fetchScannerCandidates.ts`'s `parseNumericField` call sites if the real payload shape differs. **2026-09-05 code review before the first scheduled run**: found and fixed two real issues in this untested-live path. (1) `enrichScannerCandidates.ts`'s `onTick` only accepted real-time tick IDs (bid=1/ask=2/last=4) — missing the delayed-data equivalents (66/67/68) every other IBKR call site in this codebase already handles, the exact bug class behind the 2026-08-31 trade-alert outage, reintroduced in this brand-new file; fixed to match `fetchLivePrices.ts`/`fetchTickerOverview.ts`'s existing pattern. (2) the raw `benchmark`/`projection` strings were captured but discarded before ever being logged, so a wrong-looking `iv_vs_hist_ratio` on the job's first live run would have had no diagnostic trail — `run-daily-screener-scan-job.ts` now logs a 3-sample raw value per scan code. Check Heroku logs after the next 11:00 PM UTC run for those samples. **Heroku Scheduler entry added 2026-09-05** — `job:daily-screener-scan` (`node dist/scripts/run-daily-screener-scan-job.js`), daily at 11:00 PM UTC, Basic dyno (matching the other jobs — the "Eco" note elsewhere in this doc is stale, all 7 scheduler jobs actually run Basic). Cleanly spaced after the other IBKR-touching evening jobs (market-data 9:00 PM, pnl-snapshot 9:30 PM, watchdog 10:30 PM UTC). No new config vars needed. First real run pending (job shows "Never" run as of setup) — confirm `job_runs` after it fires.
- **Screener UI polish + empty-results/sectors-dropdown bugs found and fixed 2026-09-09.** Marcelo reported the Screener tab returning nothing and its Sectors dropdown only ever showing one option. Sectors bug: `ScreenerTab.tsx` derived dropdown options from the currently-loaded/filtered `rows` instead of an independent source — self-referential, collapsed further with every filter applied. Fixed with a new `GET /screener/sectors` endpoint (`DISTINCT sector FROM screener_scan_results`, independent of the active filter). Empty-results bug: not a query bug — `screener_scan_results` had 0 rows because `job:daily-screener-scan` was scheduled at 11:00 PM UTC, 3 hours after the US market close, and IBKR's `reqScannerSubscription` only computes rankings during the live regular trading session (confirmed live: `enriched: 0` across all 3 scan codes on the 2026-09-08 run). Rescheduled to 6:00 PM UTC (2:00 PM ET, 2hr buffer before close) via the Heroku Scheduler dashboard (no CLI path for editing an existing job's time). Also shipped the rest of Marcelo's UI list: all 6 numeric filter inputs resized to fit realistic digit counts (was full `col-md-2/3` width), greyed `e.g. …` placeholder hints per field, filter state now persists to `localStorage` (`iorio-screener-last-filters`, matching `useColumnVisibility`'s convention), new Reset button, "Apply Filters" renamed to "Search" and un-full-widthed. Verified live via Playwright (both themes, desktop + mobile, localStorage persistence survives a reload, Reset clears it). **Follow-up investigation, resolved 2026-09-09**: Marcelo asked whether any market-data-type parameter could make the scan itself return real data outside market hours. Tested empirically (`tmp/testFrozenScanner.ts`, run ~8 AM ET pre-market): requested `REALTIME`, `FROZEN`, `DELAYED`, and `DELAYED_FROZEN` in turn, then ran all 3 real scan codes under each — every combination returned identical "no items retrieved" (IBKR error 165, Historical Market Data Service) for all 3. A control run of a plain price-based scan (`TOP_PERC_GAIN`, no options data) at the same moment returned 10 real rows, confirming the scanner subsystem itself works pre-market — it's specifically these option-volume/option-IV scan codes that come back empty, and no data-type parameter changes that. Root cause: US options exchanges have no pre/post-market session at all (unlike the underlying stocks, which trade on ARCA/IEX outside 9:30 AM–4:00 PM ET) — there's no options activity anywhere for these scans to rank outside RTH, full stop. So this isn't fixable by request parameters; only running within regular trading hours works, which the 6:00 PM UTC → now-corrected schedule above already does. Separately, whether `FROZEN` is worth using for the *enrichment* step (`reqMktData` in `enrichCandidate`, and other jobs' snapshot calls) if one of those ever needs to run off-hours is still untested and still worth the broader audit **Marcelo asked for**: inspect every IBKR-touching endpoint/job (`run-daily-market-data-job.ts`, `run-daily-pnl-snapshot-job.ts`, `runTradeAlertGeneration.ts`, etc.) for which ones could switch from `REALTIME` to `FROZEN` to avoid missing data outside market hours — particularly the scheduled jobs, since those run at a fixed clock time regardless of market state. Not started.

Verified live by temporarily flipping a real dev order to `submitted`. (2) **Adaptive priority picker built** — corrected an error in my own earlier analysis first: Adaptive orders were already wired in 2026-08-31 (always-on, hardcoded `"Normal"`), not missing as I initially told Marcelo. What was actually missing — a per-order Urgent/Normal/Patient picker — is now built: `OrderRequestPayload.adaptivePriority` (optional, `ibkrGatewayOrderPayload.ts`, no migration needed — `payload` is jsonb), set at confirm time via `POST /orders/:id/confirm` (validated server-side against the 3 allowed values) rather than at build time, so one picker on `OrderReviewPanel` covers every order type (open/close/roll) without threading it through three separate build endpoints. `ibkrGatewayWorker.ts`'s `buildAdaptiveAlgoFields()` now reads it, defaulting to "Normal" when absent (old rows, or Roll/Close orders that don't set it yet). Verified live: picker renders and is selectable on a real Order Review panel; **confirmed working against a real live fill** (Marcelo, 2026-09-17). (3) **"Last refreshed" timestamp shown, `TradeAlertsPage.tsx`** — the data already existed end-to-end (`trade_alerts.last_refreshed_at`, added 2026-08-24) but was never rendered anywhere. Added a relative-time label ("Refreshed 5d ago", full timestamp on hover) next to each ticker's Refresh button, computed from the max of every alert's `lastRefreshedAt ?? createdAt` in that group. Verified live against real dev data — correctly surfaced alerts genuinely 5 days stale, exactly Juan's complaint. Auto-refresh cadence (the other half of item 3) is a real decision, not built — see below. (4/6) **POP added to Order Review, `OrderReviewPanel.tsx`** — new `src/lib/probabilityOfProfit.ts` (frontend port of the backend's `blackScholesPop.ts`, same formula, computed client-side against the already-streaming live quote rather than adding a second IBKR round-trip). Deliberately does **not** reuse `liveYield`'s strike-as-spot-price fallback for orders with no stock leg (CSP, or a covered call sold against already-held shares) — POP is sensitive enough to spot-vs-breakeven distance that this would silently show a materially wrong number in exactly the case Juan called out as most important ("This is quite important for CSP specially"). Instead only renders once a real spot price is available: the order's own stock leg, or a new `liveSpotPrice` prop threaded from `TickerDetailModal`'s already-live overview stream (free — no new connection). Roll/Close don't get POP yet (no live spot on hand there without opening a second stream). Tooltip marks it "Provisional -- still being validated against IBKR's own number." **Real pre-existing bug found and fixed live-testing this**: `dte` in `OrderReviewPanel.tsx` was computed from the option leg's raw IBKR `YYYYMMDD` expiry string without converting to ISO first — `daysToExpiry` expects ISO and silently returns `NaN` on `"20260911"`. This meant the live-recomputed "Ann. Yield" figure (shipped 2026-08-27, meant to update per quote tick) has shown "—" instead of a real number since launch, never caught because it degrades silently rather than erroring. Fixed using the same `ibkrExpiryToIsoDate` conversion `legDescription()` already applied correctly one line above. Verified live: both Ann. Yield and POP now show real numbers (e.g. 175.7% yield / 69.7% POP on a real AAOI CSP). Return/Risk (the other half of item 4/6) is a new metric with no formula yet — not built, needs sign-off, see below. All three touched screens passed a `/ui-ux` review (desktop + mobile + dark theme verified live for the new controls; one pre-existing `btn-sm`-in-card-header flagged in `TradeAlertsPage.tsx:320`, not touched). Both repos typecheck clean, backend test suite passes (36/36). Adaptive priority since deployed to the VPS worker and **confirmed against a real live fill** (Marcelo, 2026-09-17); everything else is web-dyno-only and live. **Decisions resolved 2026-09-02, remaining three items built same day:**
- **Trade-alert auto-refresh cadence: skipped** (Marcelo's call — rate-limit risk not worth it right now). Only the "last refreshed" timestamp above ships; no auto-refresh loop was built.

Wired into `runTradeAlertGeneration.ts`'s existing roll-scan loop (same open-short-legs source, no new query). New nullable `position_legs.assignment_risk_notified_at` (migration `20260902000001`, additive) gates one notification per crossing — set when delta crosses into the zone, cleared once confirmed back out, so a future crossing notifies again. New `assignmentRiskBatchReady` event mirrors the existing `rollBatchReady` pattern exactly; Telegram wired into `run-trade-alert-generation-job.ts` the same way. **Verified live end-to-end against real IBKR data**: triggered a real "Run Alerts Now" full scan (17 tickers + 5 open short legs, ~5.5 min, completed `success`, zero console/job errors) — COIN's real open $177.50P leg was correctly flagged (`assignment_risk_notified_at` set from a real live delta read), the other four legs correctly stayed unflagged. That COIN flag is real signal, not test data — left in place. **Not yet verified**: the actual Telegram message text (manual "Run Now" doesn't send Telegram by design — only the scheduled job does; next real scheduled run, or a deliberate prod trigger, will exercise that path) and the re-notify-after-clearing behavior (needs delta to actually cross back out on a future run, can't be forced).

- **Genosuke gains Jack's Cloudflare WAF + Heroku-log tools — built and API-verified live 2026-09-01, not yet in prod.** Marcelo asked to bring menaris-admin-api's "Jack" WAF-blocking and log-reading tools into Genosuke. New `src/lib/cloudflareService.ts`/`src/lib/betterstackService.ts` (ported from Jack's `cloudflare-service.js`/`betterstack-service.js`, adapted to this codebase's plain-`fetch` convention). Three new Genosuke tools: `list_waf_rules`/`fetch_logs` (read tier) and a new **`infra-write` tier** (`add_waf_rule`/`remove_waf_rule`/`unblock_ip`) — deliberately reuses the same real Telegram Yes/Cancel confirm gate as `financial-write` rather than Jack's prompt-only "ask before acting" convention (consistent with the reasoning already documented in `financialWriteTools.ts` for why that was judged too weak a guarantee). `fetch_logs` takes a required `sourceApp: "api"|"app"` since `iorio-reloaded-api` and `iorio-reloaded-app` landed in two separate Better Stack teams (`t591705`/`t591706`) when the Logtail add-on was provisioned, each with its own SQL credentials — not one shared account as first assumed. **Two real bugs found and fixed via direct testing against live Cloudflare** (`tmp/testCloudflareBetterstack.ts`, RFC 5737 reserved test IPs, cleaned up after): (1) `ioriore.com` is a brand-new zone with no WAF ruleset ever created (unlike menaris's zone, which already had one) — the ported code threw on Cloudflare error 10003 instead of creating the ruleset on first use; fixed via `PUT` to the entrypoint instead of assuming `POST .../rules` always has a ruleset id to target. (2) once the ruleset exists but drops to zero rules (e.g. right after the last IP is unblocked), Cloudflare omits the `rules` field from the response entirely instead of returning `[]`, crashing `listWafRules()`'s `.map()` — normalized in `getCustomRuleset()`. Full write path verified live end-to-end: single-IP block, second IP merging into the same shared blocklist rule (Free-plan 5-rule cap), partial unblock (one IP removed, rule survives), full unblock (rule deleted), and a non-IP path-based rule added/removed by id — zone confirmed back to zero rules after. **Not yet done**: `CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN`/`CLOUDFLARE_ZONE_ID`/`BETTERSTACK_SQL_*`/`BETTERSTACK_APP_SQL_*` aren't in Heroku prod config yet (local `.env` only) — needs `heroku config:set`, which Marcelo has to run himself (classifier blocks Claude from `heroku` commands entirely). The Genosuke chat path itself (tool calls arriving through a real Telegram message, not calling the service functions directly) also hasn't been exercised yet.
- **Dashboard "Day P&L shows nonzero while WTD shows $0" bug, found and fixed 2026-09-01.** Reported live from a screenshot: Residual showed −$5,083 Day but $0 WTD, which shouldn't be possible if Day ⊆ Week. Root cause: `strategyPeriodPnl.ts`'s `day_start` was `MAX(snapshot_date)` (a fix from a *different* bug, 2026-08-28) — on a normal Monday, before that day's own snapshot has run, this resolves to last Friday, which falls *before* `week_start` (this Monday), so Friday's already-reported realized trades leaked back into Monday's Day figure while Week correctly excluded them (not just a job-outage edge case — this happened every Monday). Fix, both approved by Marcelo: (1) new `market_calendar` table (migration `20260901000002`), synced from MarketData.app's free market-status API (`scripts/sync-market-calendar.ts`, `npm run sync-market-calendar` — no scheduled job, re-run whenever forward coverage runs low; free-tier signup, no card, token in `MARKETDATA_API_TOKEN`) — `day_start` now anchors on the last real trading day from this table (falls back to a weekday-only rule if unsynced), decoupling it from the snapshot job's own health. (2) Day's realized/unrealized boundaries flipped to exclusive (`>`/`<=` instead of `>=`/`<`) since `day_start` now represents the last *completed* session itself, not the start of the current one like week/month/year do — stops re-counting that prior session's trades entirely. Verified: SQL runs clean against dev DB, real MarketData.app holiday data spot-checked against known 2026 NYSE holidays (Thanksgiving, Christmas) and matches. **Not yet done**: deploy to prod (migration + `MARKETDATA_API_TOKEN` config var already set on Heroku; needs push + one manual `heroku run "npm run sync-market-calendar"` after deploy, since `heroku run` is blocked from this session).

(1) **Fixed.** `.flash-changed` was never actually applied to a… *(full entry archived: PROGRESS-ARCHIVE.md)*
- **Worker verbose logging shipped 2026-09-01 (`a4b0349`), watching for recurrence of unexplained restarts/stalls.** Triggered by a real AAOI position bug caught live: after the $119 covered call expired and a new $115 call was sold against the same 100 shares, the app briefly showed AAOI twice — the old "Needs Review" position and the new Covered Call both still claiming the same 100 real shares (confirmed via direct DB query: both `position_legs` rows shared the identical IBKR contract id, so it was one real lot double-recorded, not 200 phantom shares). The self-healing cleanup code that's supposed to close the stale leg every 60s reconciliation pass (`upsertLeftoverStockPosition`) was already correct and already deployed — it just took ~90 reconciliation cycles (~90 min) instead of 1 to actually fire, with zero errors logged in between. Root cause not fully confirmed, but the strongest candidate found by code review: `runReconciliationPass`'s `if (!ib) return` early-exit (hit whenever `getIb()` is briefly null mid-reconnect) previously logged nothing at all, so a long stretch of silently-skipped passes was indistinguishable from a healthy quiet period in the logs. This is also the same instability class already logged once before (2026-08-27 entry below, "worker silently disconnected for 8+ hours") — the worker has needed several unexplained restarts/day per Marcelo, never root-caused. Rather than restart-and-hope (explicitly rejected — "that would kill the chance we have now to find a real bug"), added verbose logging across the worker instead: every reconciliation pass now logs start/end+duration, mutex block/queue/rerun events, per-symbol branch decisions with actual numbers (`stockQty`, `shortCallLegs`, `leftoverShares`, `isCoveredCall`), every `upsertLeftoverStockPosition`/`upsertSplitCoveredCallPosition` call with what it found and did, and which legs get closed each pass and why. `ibkrGatewayPersistentConnection.ts` now also logs every IBKR system-status code (1100-series connectivity notices, market-data-farm codes) that was previously discarded silently on `reqId === -1`, connection timing on every (re)connect, and connection uptime + last-known system-status code on every disconnect. Added a 5-minute heartbeat log (connection state, uptime, lifetime reconnects, reconciliation pass count, mutex state) so a genuinely hung process is now distinguishable from a quiet-but-healthy one. Deployed and confirmed live (`npm run deploy:worker`, restarted clean, first pass's output readable and correct: `AAOI — stockQty=100, shortCallLegs=1 ..., isCoveredCall=true, leftoverShares=0` → correctly a no-op). Deliberately verbose for now, while still actively developing — plan is to trim it down once the worker's proven stable for a real stretch, not before. **Not yet done**: actually catching a live recurrence with the new logging in place to confirm the root cause theory above.
- **`PositionCard.tsx`'s per-leg EXIT column falsy-zero bug, fixed 2026-09-01.** Rendered `leg.exitPrice ? formatCurrency(...) : "—"` — since a worthless option expiry is recorded as `exit_price: 0`, the truthy check treated a legitimate $0 exit the same as "no exit data," always showing "–" for an expired-worthless leg even though `exit_at` was correctly set. Fixed to check `leg.exitAt` instead. Found while investigating the AAOI bug above — the leg table showed the expired $119 call as if still open (blank EXIT column), which is what made the "Needs Review" status look inconsistent/confusing at a glance.

- **Option chain stale-strike bug found and fixed, 2026-09-01** (`iorio-reloaded-app`). Reported live on SNDK: pending trade-alert strikes (1435/1440/1445 puts, 1575/1580/1585 calls) weren't showing in the Ticker Detail modal's Option Chain, despite `fetchOptionChain.ts`'s `mustIncludeStrikes` mechanism (2026-08-26) existing specifically to force pending-alert strikes into the near-the-money window. Confirmed against prod DB directly first (all six strikes genuinely `status='pending'`, `alert_type='new_trade'`), ruling out a data/lifecycle explanation before touching code. Root cause: `streamTickerDetail.ts` computes which strikes to force-include only once, at SSE connection open — it never re-runs for the life of that connection. The chain being viewed had opened before a scheduled `runTradeAlertGeneration.ts` regen (16:45:42) superseded the alerts it was built from; the alerts table (a separate fetch, refetched on Scan/Refresh and order fill) correctly showed the new pending strikes while the chain kept showing the now-expired ones — see [[project_ticker_detail_stream_frozen_at_open]].

  Fix, two parts, both Playwright-verified live on local dev end-to-end. (1) Explicit triggers: `TickerDetailModal.tsx` now bumps a `streamKey` after Scan/Refresh completes and after an order fills, tearing down and reopening the whole ticker-detail stream (overview+chart+chain together, one shared IBKR connection) so it recomputes must-include strikes from the current alert list. (2) Background regen — the scheduled job firing while the modal just sits open, no user action: a 60s poll compares the pending-alert strike signature against what's loaded; if it changed while the user has an order selection/review panel open, the chain deliberately does *not* reconnect (would blow away their in-progress selection) — instead shows a dismissible toast ("Trade alerts updated... the chain will refresh once you finish this order") with a "Refresh now" override, and auto-reconnects the moment the panel closes. Verified by triggering a real backend regen directly (bypassing the Refresh button, so the frontend found out only via the poll) while an order was mid-build: selection survived, toast appeared, and closing the panel triggered the reconnect with the chain repainting the new strikes. Both repos typecheck clean.

- **IV Rank + IV Percentile backend built and verified against real dev DB, 2026-08-31 — backend only, no frontend yet.** Follow-up to the IV history coverage test above and the deferred "IV history chart" idea (P/L Split & Roll Intelligence Phase 2). New `daily_price_bars.implied_volatility` column (migration `20260831000001`) populated two ways: the daily job (`run-daily-market-data-job.ts`) now also fetches the latest `OPTION_IMPLIED_VOLATILITY` daily bar alongside the existing OHLCV one; `priceBarCache.ts`'s 1Y/5Y/All backfill/top-up (triggered by opening a ticker's chart) now fetches the same IV series over the same duration and merges it in. Both merges use `COALESCE(excluded, existing)` specifically so a transient IV-fetch failure can't null out an already-cached value. New shared `src/lib/ivMetrics.ts` (`computeIvMetrics`) — **two metrics, both approved 2026-08-31, deliberately from the same source** so they can't drift apart: **IV Rank** = `(today − 252d min) / (252d max − 252d min) × 100` (existing approved formula, migrated here — see below) and **IV Percentile** = `% of the other days in the last 252 whose IV closed below today's` (new, chosen over Rank alone because a single outlier day like an old earnings spike permanently skews Rank's min/max but not Percentile). Wired into `AlertCandidate` (both `ivRank`/`ivPercentile` fields, flowing through `generateTradeAlertCandidates`/`generateTradeAlertCandidatesForTicker`/`refreshTradeAlert`) and into `GET /screener`. **Real bug caught mid-build**: Screener already had an approved, shipped IV Rank column sourced from a *different* table (`market_data_snapshots`, the live daily-tick capture) — hadn't been checked before proposing IV Percentile on Trade Alerts from `daily_price_bars` instead, which would have left two silently-divergent IV numbers on two screens. Caught before shipping; Marcelo's call was to migrate Screener's IV Rank onto `daily_price_bars` too rather than leave the two sources split, so both metrics on both screens now read the same history. Verified end-to-end against real dev DB: backfilled real 1Y IV history for AAOI/MU/DELL (`tmp/backfillAndVerifyIvMetrics.ts`), confirmed sane divergent Rank/Percentile numbers (MU: 16.1/7.2, DELL: 61.4/70.4), and confirmed AAOI's `0.0/0.0` reading against the raw data directly — its most recent IV really is this ticker's 1-year low, not a bug. Hit `GET /screener` live via curl (real session login) and confirmed `ivRank`/`ivPercentile`/`ivWindowDays` render correctly, `null` for tickers with no IV history yet. Both repos typecheck clean, existing test suite passes. **Frontend since built and committed** (`iorio-reloaded-app`, confirmed 2026-09-01 via clean working tree at `4526986` "Fix IV History chart squished on initial render"): Screener's IV Rank/IV %ile columns, Trade Alerts' inline IV on both new-trade and roll alerts, and `IvHistoryChart.tsx` (alongside the price chart in `TickerDetailModal`, 1Y/5Y/All range toggle) are all live — this entry was stale claiming otherwise. **Still not yet done**: Trade Alerts' `ivRank`/`ivPercentile` fields haven't been exercised against a real live candidate scan (needs market hours for the delta/premium quotes the rest of that pipeline depends on).

- **Modal/action wiring audit — full app-wide audit run 2026-08-31 after Marcelo noticed Dashboard's "Sell Call" button opened a modal with no visible way to actually sell a call. All tiers now shipped**, including Tier 3 (consolidate `PositionDetailModal`/`TickerDetailModal` into one modal). Marcelo explicitly flagged this as critical, money-adjacent work needing exhaustive testing, not spot-checks — testing notes below are deliberately thorough.
  - **Tier 1a — the actual bug: Sell Call had no guaranteed path to an order.** `TickerDetailModal`'s order-builder only rendered after clicking a *trade alert* row/pill (`selectedAlert` state) — clicking a raw option-chain strike did nothing, even though the API's `sourceAlertId` is optional. A ticker with no matching alert at that moment (timing/ranking) made "Sell Call" a dead end: the only available action was "Scan for Alerts" and hope. Fixed: replaced the alert-only `selectedAlert` state with a general `ChainSelection` (`TickerDetailModal.tsx`) that can originate from either an alert (`selectAlert`) or a raw clicked quote (`selectQuote`, new) — every bid/ask/delta/yield cell in the chain is now clickable, matching an alert automatically via the existing `matchAlertToQuote` when one exists (keeping its frozen fallback values), falling back to pure live-quote data when it doesn't. Verified live: clicked an unmatched PLTR $185 put, built a real order (`source_alert_id: null` confirmed in the DB), reviewed it (correct SELL leg/price), cancelled cleanly; re-verified the alert-based path (delta fallback, expiry auto-jump) still works unchanged.
  - **Tier 1c — two destructive actions fired with no confirmation.** Screener "Remove" and Trade Blotter "Cancel" (order cancel) both executed immediately on click. New shared `ConfirmModal.tsx` (doesn't close on backdrop/ESC, per the app's action-modal convention) wired into both. Verified live in both flows (backed out via the modal's own Cancel each time); re-verified again after the Tier 3 merge landed — untouched, still working.
  - **On-demand roll-candidate generation** — prerequisite for a working Roll button anywhere Roll isn't already tied to a live Trade Alert. New `src/ibkr/evaluateRollForPosition.ts` (`evaluateRollForPosition(positionId, legId)`) reuses the existing `evaluateRollCandidate` (`generateRollCandidates.ts`) with a new `force: true` option that computes a replacement candidate even when the 50%-decay/21-DTE thresholds haven't actually fired — a new `stillTriggered` field on the result (also flows through to the batch job's alerts, always `true` there) lets the UI show "you're rolling early" instead of implying a real trigger. New read-only `POST /positions/:id/roll-candidate` route (writes nothing — no `trade_alerts`/`order_requests` row). `POST /:id/roll` had `sourceAlertId` relaxed to optional (was hard-required), mirroring `POST /orders`'s existing pattern. `RollPositionModal`'s prop type widened from requiring a full `TradeAlert` to `{id?, symbol, relatedPositionId, suggestedStructure}`, with a "rolling early" warning banner when `stillTriggered === false`.
  - **Tier 3 — consolidation, built and shipped.** New `PositionCard.tsx` (extracted from the old `PositionDetailModal`: legs table, payoff chart, editable Price Target/Close Trigger Notes/Notes, live P&L badges) renders inside `TickerDetailModal`, one card per currently-open position for that symbol (handles the "a symbol can have >1 concurrent open position" case by construction — a `.map()` over the open-positions array — though no real dev data currently has two, so this specific path is code-correct but not exercised live). A collapsed "Show History (N)" section below lists closed positions read-only (strategy, structure, opened/closed dates, realized P&L) — this is what closes gap #8 (Trade Blotter's closed-row dead end). Roll is new on each open short-option-leg row: click → `fetchRollCandidate` → `RollPositionModal`. Close still opens the real `ClosePositionModal` (Tier 1b resolved as a side effect — the old weaker inline close no longer exists, so there's only ever one Close implementation). `PositionDetailModal.tsx` deleted; every prior call site (Positions' Symbol + Notes columns, Dashboard's Needs Attention ticker + Sell Call) now opens `TickerDetailModal` with a new `focusPositionId` prop that scrolls the right card into view. New `GET /positions?symbol=X&status=all` backend filter feeds it (one call for both open and closed positions). Risk & Limits' Concentration-by-Ticker list (Tier 2, previously the one screen with zero click-through) now opens the same modal too; Concentration-by-Sector deliberately stays plain text (not a real ticker).
  - **Exhaustive live-test pass, 2026-08-31**: all 8 entry points confirmed opening the correct symbol/position (Positions Symbol, Positions Notes, Positions Sell Call, Dashboard Needs Attention ticker, Dashboard Needs Attention Sell Call, Screener, Trade Alerts ticker + "Review"/`initialAlertId` still auto-jumps and opens the order panel correctly alongside the new Position section, Trade Blotter, Price Performance, Calendar, and the new Risk & Limits click-through — Concentration-by-Sector confirmed to correctly stay non-clickable). A symbol with zero positions (GS) confirmed to render neither section, no crash. **Close, tested end-to-end for real**: opened COIN's (3x short put) Close Position from inside the merged modal, filled a limit price, reviewed — Order Review correctly showed "BUY 3x $177.50 Put... @ $0.10" — cancelled; confirmed `cancelled` in the DB, nothing dangling. **Roll, tested 5 times against 5 different real open short legs** (MU, DRAM, INTC, and 2 legs on COIN) — every attempt hit the same weekend/delayed-data pricing-snapshot timeout already documented elsewhere in this project (not a bug: `lookupPricingSnapshot`'s existing 10s-timeout behavior, now correctly surfaced as an inline alert instead of a generic error or a crash each time; the button re-enabled cleanly after every failure). **A real `status: "ok"` roll candidate was never produced this session — that path needs a real retry during market hours before being called fully verified.** Confirmed on a mixed-leg COIN position that the Roll button correctly appeared only on the still-open short put, not the already-exited leg sitting in the same position's leg history — exercises the `!leg.exitAt` eligibility filter on real messy data. Mobile (390px): confirmed no page-level horizontal overflow; the legs table scrolls inside its own `.table-responsive` container (1188px content in a 372px viewport) per the established convention. Dark theme confirmed via computed styles (modal/card background `rgb(42,54,72)`, text `rgb(249,250,251)`); light theme confirmed white background/dark text. Zero orphaned `order_requests` rows in any non-terminal status from the entire session's testing, confirmed via direct DB query.
- **Close an unstructured position from the app — designed and built 2026-08-31, both repos typecheck clean, verified against real dev DB (curl) and live via Playwright (light/dark/mobile).** Followed up on the "Open, not yet built" item below. Design confirmed with Marcelo before implementation: full per-leg generalization (not just bare-stock), partial close allowed, multi-leg closes go out as a single BAG combo (matching the existing atomicity decision) rather than separate orders. **Real live bug found and fixed along the way**: `ClosePositionModal`'s existing `contractsToClose` path always derived stock quantity as `contractsToClose × multiplier` off a single option leg — for an unstructured position like AAOI (200 sh + 1 leftover short call, mismatched ratio), hitting the old Close button would have silently closed only 100 of the 200 shares. Fixed by branching the close route (`POST /positions/:id/close`, `src/routes/positions.ts`) on `strategyKey === "unstructured"`: that branch accepts an explicit `quantity` per leg in the `legs[]` payload instead of deriving one, allows any subset of open legs (partial close), and validates each leg independently — structured `covered_call`/`cash_secured_put` closes are completely unchanged (same `contractsToClose`-derives-everything path as before). Nothing needed to change in the worker or reconciliation — `POST /positions/:id/close` was already leg-agnostic downstream; confirmed the worker's reconciliation pass (not this route) is what decides whether a position is fully closed, so a partial close correctly leaves remaining legs open. Frontend: `ClosePositionModal.tsx` now branches on `strategyKey`, rendering one row per open leg (include checkbox, quantity capped at held, its own limit price) for unstructured positions; `PositionsPage.tsx`'s actions column now shows Close for any open unstructured position (previously bare-stock-only unstructured positions had no close path at all), alongside Sell Call when there's an open stock leg. Verified end-to-end: curl against real dev DB (partial 50-of-200-share AAOI close built correctly, over-quantity and invalid-legId requests correctly rejected, structured CSP close path unaffected), then the same partial close clicked through the real UI (Order Review correctly showed "SELL 50 sh @ $1.23"), in both light and dark themes and at 390px mobile width. All test orders cancelled afterward, nothing left dangling in `order_requests`. **Not yet exercised**: a real multi-leg unstructured close (e.g. naked call + mismatched stock) — today's only real unstructured positions are single-leg bare stock, so the BAG-combo path for this shape is code-reviewed/tested via the existing structured multi-leg path but not fired with a real unstructured multi-leg position yet.

- **Real-time market data + account-access architecture — researched against IBKR's own documentation 2026-08-31 (support's phone explanations were contradictory and not trusted at face value), playbook artifact handed to Juan, root cause narrowed conclusively via direct testing same day.** Two architecture conclusions reached with high confidence, sourced directly from IBKR docs (not support): (1) Support's claim that connecting the sub-account disconnects the main account is **false as stated** — IBKR's real rule is one active session per *username*, not per account; two different usernames on the same account (e.g. Juan's own login + the bot's) can be connected simultaneously, which is IBKR's documented pattern for running an unattended API login alongside a human login. The disconnect behavior support described is real but only applies to the *same* username logged in twice (confirmed by Juan's own Paper Trading Account screen, which states this explicitly for a live username and its own paper twin). (2) **Confirmed via Users & Access Rights screenshot**: `iorio-bot10` and `johndom873` are both listed under the same account `U18059120` — a second username, not a separate sub-account, as suspected.

  **Real-time data root cause narrowed by direct elimination, not guesswork**: ran `tmp/checkRealtimeDataEntitlement.ts` against the VPS paper Gateway three times, each ruling out one candidate cause: (a) tested with no live session running at all → rules out the live/paper simultaneous-session restriction as the current blocker; (b) tested against both a NASDAQ ticker (AAPL) and an NYSE ticker (KO), identical `error 10089` on both → rules out a per-exchange subscription-bundle gap; (c) hard-restarted the paper Gateway container for a fully fresh session, confirmed "Login has completed"/"Configuration tasks completed" in its logs, re-tested → still identical error → rules out a stale session that hadn't picked up the sharing change. Sharing is correctly configured (Yes, sourced from `johndom873`) and the setting is well past IBKR's 24h propagation window. **Remaining explanation, not yet confirmed**: `johndom873` itself most likely does not hold an actual active/approved real-time market data subscription — the sharing toggle only pipes an existing entitlement through, it doesn't grant one. **Next step**: Juan to check Client Portal → Settings → **Market Data Subscriptions** (not the Paper Trading Account screen) under `johndom873`, looking for the subscription's actual status (Active vs. Pending vs. never submitted).

  **Root cause confirmed 2026-08-31** via Juan's Market Data Subscriptions screenshot for `johndom873`: the account was never actually subscribed to real exchange-level streaming data. Subscribed list is PAXOS Crypto, US Mutual Funds, "US Real-Time Non Consolidated Streaming Quotes," and the "US Securities Snapshot and Futures Value Bundle" (snapshot-only, not continuous streaming) — but **no "NYSE (Network A/CTA)" and no "Nasdaq (Network C/UTP)"**, which are the specific subscriptions that deliver continuous top-of-book streaming quotes per exchange and are exactly what the API's `10089` error was asking for (`AAPL NASDAQ.NMS/TOP/ALL`, `KO NYSE/TOP/ALL`). Not a sharing/session/sub-account bug at all — sharing, the account architecture, and the gateway session were all confirmed correct through the elimination pass above; the underlying entitlement itself was simply never purchased. **Fix, not yet applied**: Juan to add both subscriptions via the gear icon on that page (~$1.50/mo each, typically fee-waived once monthly commissions clear the same ~$30 threshold as the existing snapshot bundle); re-test `tmp/checkRealtimeDataEntitlement.ts` after they're active and past propagation.

  **Correction 2026-08-31, before Juan acted on the fix above**: NYSE (Network A/CTA) and Nasdaq (Network C/UTP) are equity/stock tape feeds only — they do not cover option contracts. Real-time US option chain data (the actual dependency for POP/greeks/IV in the covered-call/CSP strategies) is a separate subscription, **OPRA (US Options Exchanges)**, administered independently of the equity networks industry-wide, not an IBKR-specific quirk. Juan added all three (NASDAQ Network C, NYSE Network A, OPRA, confirmed via screenshot).

  **Stock-side real-time data confirmed fixed and live, 2026-08-31.** After adding the three subscriptions, `tmp/checkRealtimeDataEntitlement.ts` still failed with a new, different error — `10197 "No market data during competing live session"` — which is the exact live/paper simultaneous-session restriction IBKR's own Paper Trading Account page warns about (a live username and its own paper twin can't both hold an active session). Root cause: Juan had forgotten to log out of `johndom873` in Client Portal after checking his subscriptions, and no live Gateway container was running on the VPS (confirmed — only the paper container was up), so the competing session was Juan's own browser/app login, not our infrastructure. Once he logged out, re-ran the test: **AAPL and KO both now report `marketDataType: 1 (REAL-TIME)` directly from IBKR** — confirmed, not inferred.

  **OPRA (option contract) real-time data confirmed too, 2026-08-31.** New `tmp/checkRealtimeOptionEntitlement.ts` (sibling to the stock version — resolves a real, currently-listed option contract via `resolveContractId`/`lookupOptionParams` rather than guessing strike/expiry, then does the same `marketDataType` tick check) run against AAPL: a real $287.50C expiring 2026-08-31 reports `marketDataType: 1 (REAL-TIME)` directly from IBKR. **This entire thread is now fully resolved** — architecture, sharing config, and both the equity and options real-time feeds all confirmed working end-to-end, not just configured.

  **Competing-live-session detection built and typechecked 2026-08-31** (Marcelo approved same session): `checkIbkrHealthJob.ts`'s `competingLiveSessionIsBlockingData()` probes SPY with `reqMarketDataType(REALTIME)` and watches specifically for IBKR error `10197` on that request — the same signature confirmed twice manually earlier this session. Deliberately notify-only, not restart-triggering (a Gateway restart doesn't fix this — it's a live-session state issue on `johndom873`, not a Gateway problem), same pattern as the existing reconciliation-problem findings. `job_runs.details.competingLiveSession` now recorded on every run. Verified live: ran immediately after Juan logged out of `johndom873` and got `competingLiveSession: false`, confirmed via direct DB query — the false-path is proven working end to end. **Not yet re-verified**: the true-positive path (would need Juan to log back into `johndom873` briefly to watch it fire through this exact code), though the check reuses the identical `reqMarketDataType`/`reqMktData`/error-10197 pattern already confirmed twice via manual testing earlier the same session. **Since pushed** (`d87b811` confirmed on `origin/main` as of 2026-09-01) — live in prod's Heroku Scheduler job. **Still not yet done**: re-verifying the true-positive path through this exact code (only the `false`/clear case has been confirmed live so far) — would need Juan to log into `johndom873` again briefly; optional given the check reuses an error signature already confirmed twice manually.
