  Design decisions (all shipped as designed, no changes from the original plan):
  - **Position valuation = full market value across all legs, platform-wide** — see [[project_position_valuation_full_market_value]]. New shared `src/lib/positionExposure.ts` (`computePositionExposures()`, `computeCashLockedInCsps()`) replaces `riskLimits.ts`'s old stock-leg-only/option-leg-only CTE; `riskLimits.ts` refactored to a thin `groupByKey` wrapper around the shared function so concentration/allocation/top-positions stay mutually consistent. Fixes the live bug where a stock-only unstructured position had no option leg to price and silently vanished from every allocation/concentration view.
  - **Per-strategy Day/WTD/MTD/YTD + the multi-series chart, computed live at query time** — new `src/lib/strategyPeriodPnl.ts` (`computeStrategyPeriodPnl()`, `computeStrategyDailyPnlSeries()`), no new snapshot table, per the original plan.
  - **`positions.close_reason` / `positions.unstructured_reason`** (new columns) + new narrow `platform_anomalies` table, all in migration `20260828000001_add_close_and_unstructured_reasons.ts`. `ibkrGatewayWorker.ts`'s `determineCloseReason()`/`determineLeftoverStockReason()` compute and persist these once per reconciliation pass instead of recomputing transiently; a `logPlatformAnomaly()` helper writes a row whenever a close or leftover-stock case can't be explained. Final enums shipped: close reasons `assigned` / `expired_worthless` / `closed_via_app` / `closed_via_external_trade` / `unknown`; unstructured reasons `cc_expired_leftover_stock` / `csp_assigned_stock` / `unknown` (a leftover stock position with a naked short call alongside it is always `unknown` — the platform should never produce that shape). **Untested against real data** — no unstructured position has appeared since this shipped, so only the `expired_worthless`/`assigned` close-reason paths (reused from the pre-existing `notifyPositionExpired` heuristic) have exercised live; the two `unstructured_reason` branches and both anomaly-logging paths have only been read-through. Worth a deliberate live test or a dry run against historical data once the worker's deployed.
  - **Other → split into Unstructured + Residual**, Residual as a single plug figure (`account_pnl_snapshots` trusted total minus CC+CSP+Unstructured), shipped as designed. Flex Query breakdown of Residual still deferred/undecided, not part of this pass.
  - **Events feed** — new `src/lib/positionEvents.ts` (`fetchPositionEvents()`), reconstructed from `positions`/`position_legs`/`trades`/`order_requests` with no new events table, confirming the original plan. Renders grouped by strategy with per-event-type detail (opened/closed/unstructured), a relative-timestamp display, and a User column (best-effort attribution — see below). Iterated twice after first ship: Value column switched from net-cash-effect to full market value at entry/exit (same standard as Portfolio), and a real display bug fixed where an "opened" event was showing the leg's *exit* price instead of entry price for legs that had since closed.

  **Bugs found and fixed during the build** (all confirmed root-caused, not just patched): (1) CSP cash-lock exposure — Available Cash wasn't netting out collateral locked by open CSPs correctly, fixed via `computeCashLockedInCsps()`. (2) Day column showing exactly $0.00 for per-strategy P&L on two separate occasions with two unrelated root causes — first, `day_start` anchored to literal `CURRENT_DATE` instead of the most-recently-captured snapshot date (mid-day, before the nightly job runs, "today" has no snapshot yet); second, the as-of lookup used `<=` instead of `<` against the period start, so once the nightly snapshot *had* run, "now" and "as of day start" resolved to the same row. Both fixed in `strategyPeriodPnl.ts`, documented inline. (3) A misleading "$0.00" realized P&L on closed positions whose legs exited before `exit_price` tracking was reliable — `positionEvents.ts`'s `realizedPnlFor` now returns `null` (not a false 0) whenever any leg has `exit_at` but no `exit_price`. (4) Dark-mode borders: `--tblr-border-color-translucent` (consumed by `.card`/`.table`/`.dropdown-menu`, a separate token from `--tblr-border-color` which only fixed `.nav-tabs`/`.list-group`) and `.modal-content` (ships with a transparent border, relying on a box-shadow tuned for a light backdrop that had no visible edge against this app's dark `rgba(0,0,0,0.5)` backdrop) — both fixed globally in `theme.css`, not scoped to Dashboard.

  **Parallel work note**: the same-day user-attribution work (`1f1a9c1` in this repo, `796894c` in the app repo — Trade Blotter/Trade Alerts/Risk & Limits requester/canceller names) does not overlap with or supersede the Events feed's attribution — different query surfaces (orders/alerts lists vs. reconstructed position lifecycle), same underlying join pattern (`order_requests.requested_by_user_id` → `users`). The Events feed's attribution gap (a manually-entered position with no source alert has no link back to any `order_request`, so shows no name) is a genuine data gap, not something that work fixes.

  **Shipped 2026-08-28**: both repos committed and pushed, prod migration confirmed run (`knex_migrations`), VPS worker deployed (`close_reason`/`unstructured_reason` now live in the Events feed), and a full fresh Playwright pass (light/dark, desktop/mobile) confirmed done.

## Order execution platform (started 2026-08-24)

**Decision**: IBKR is the source of truth for positions, not the app's own database — the app places real orders (paper Gateway) and continuously syncs IBKR's reported state back into Postgres. **Why**: a real prod bug (AAOI's naked covered call from a free-typed quantity field with no cross-leg validation) showed manual position entry could silently drift from reality; the fix was to remove manual entry entirely in favor of real order placement.

**What's built and verified against real paper trades** (opens, fills, a full close, a cancel of a live order, and reconciliation-driven self-healing have all been confirmed against real IBKR activity — a real roll has not yet been exercised):
- `order_requests` table — the web-to-worker order queue (`pending_confirmation → confirmed → submitted → filled|partially_filled|cancelled|rejected|error`).
- `src/ibkr/ibkrGatewayPersistentConnection.ts` — the app's one long-lived IBKR connection (fixed `clientId=42`, everywhere else uses per-request random ids), reconnects with backoff.
- `src/ibkrGatewayWorker.ts` (separate `worker:` process, deployed to the VPS not Heroku — see [[project_worker_runs_on_vps]]) — places orders (single-leg, or an atomic BAG combo for multi-leg), tracks fills, and runs `reconcilePositionsFromIbkr()` every 60s + on connect, with an in-flight mutex so passes never overlap. Pairing heuristic: long stock + short call same symbol → `covered_call`; any short put → `cash_secured_put`, handled unconditionally and independently of a covered call on the same symbol (fixed 2026-08-27 — a real AAOI position had its put stranded as `unstructured` forever after its sibling call/stock rolled off into a separate covered_call position, since the put was only ever considered when no stock/calls existed at all); anything else → `unstructured` ("Needs Review" badge, never hidden). Each distinct short-call conId now splits into its own position + proportional stock slice (fixed 2026-08-25 after MU showed 2 calls wrongly merged into one position).
- **2026-08-27: `ibkrGateway*` naming convention adopted.** Every file whose code ships as part of the VPS-deployed worker (`ibkrGatewayWorker.ts` and its `src/ibkr/ibkrGateway*.ts` imports) is prefixed `ibkrGateway`, so it's obvious at a glance which edits need a manual `npm run deploy:worker`, not just a `git push`.
- **2026-08-27: worker silently disconnected for 8+ hours, root-caused and fixed.** `ibkrGatewayWorker.ts`'s persistent IBKR connection dropped at 12:50 UTC; its reconnect loop logged one failed attempt then went silent forever, even though `systemctl status` still showed the process "active (running)". Root cause: `ibkrGatewayTunnel.ts`'s `openIbkrTunnel()` awaited `sshClient.connect()` with no timeout — a stalled SSH attempt left that promise permanently pending, freezing the reconnect loop with no further logs (distinct from the already-fixed unhandled-pipe-error crash noted below). While disconnected, IBKR-side closes on 4 MU/AAOI legs never synced back to `position_legs`, surfacing as false "position reconciliation" alerts from the separate hourly health-check connection (which uses its own short-lived connection via `connectIbkr.ts` and kept working throughout). **Fixed**: `openIbkrTunnel()` now has its own 15s timeout on the SSH connect phase. Worker restarted and redeployed same day; stale legs self-healed via the normal reconciliation pass within seconds of reconnecting, confirmed via direct DB check and the next hourly health-check run logging zero discrepancies.
- `src/routes/positions.ts` — build-then-confirm pattern everywhere: `POST /orders` (preview, writes nothing) → `POST /orders/:id/confirm` (transmit gate, `pg_notify`s the worker) → `POST /orders/:id/cancel` (works pre-submission instantly; once submitted, flips to `cancel_requested` and the worker calls `ib.cancelOrder()` — confirmed against a real live order). Roll and close follow the same pattern. Close accepts `contractsToClose`, option-leg-driven (stock quantity always derived, never independently settable).
- `src/ibkr/checkPositionReconciliation.ts` — compares every open local leg against IBKR's real holdings, flags orphans/mismatches in either direction. Wired into the `ibkr_health_check` job (every 10 min). Detection only, no auto-fix. Verified against real forced drift.
- Frontend: shared `OrderReviewPanel.tsx` (review → explicit Confirm → poll to terminal status) used by New Position, Close, and Roll. Genosuke's three order tools call build+confirm sequentially, gated by Telegram's existing Yes/Cancel.

**Root cause chain for why every order was initially rejected (all fixed, 2026-08-24)**: `READ_ONLY_API=yes` was a leftover default (fixed → `no`); IBC's automated config dialog sequence was silently interrupted by an unhandled disclaimer dialog, so the Read-Only fix never actually saved until manually clicked through once via `xdotool`; IBKR error codes 399 and 2100-2169 are informational, not rejections, and were being mishandled as such. **Residual risk closed 2026-08-24**: a bind volume for the Gateway's settings dir now survives full container recreation, not just restarts — verified against a real `docker compose down/up`.

**Recurring bug class hit repeatedly across this build, now fixed everywhere found**: IBKR reports `""`/`0` (not `null`/`undefined`) for a contract field that doesn't apply to that security type (a stock's expiry/strike, an option's multiplier under some paths) — `??` doesn't catch it, `||` does. Hit and fixed for: stock leg `expiry_date`, `strike_price`, `multiplier` (this last one silently zeroed the stock leg's entire contribution to unrealized P&L until fixed); option leg `entryPrice`'s `multiplier` divisor; `sourceAlertId` from Genosuke's manual-entry path. See [[project_ibkr_empty_string_not_null]].

**Other real bugs found via real fills/closes, all fixed**: opening fills weren't recorded into `trades` at all for a brand-new position (fixed by buffering unmatched executions and draining them once the leg exists); `execution.time`'s IBKR format (`"YYYYMMDD HH:mm:ss <IANA zone>"`) isn't parseable by `new Date()` (fixed with a dedicated parser, DST-safe); a partial closing fill was marking the whole leg closed regardless of remaining IBKR-held quantity (fixed — only the reconciliation pass's zero-holding check now closes a leg); BAG combo leg ratios needed reducing to their GCD, not sent as raw quantities (IBKR rejected a 3-contract close outright until fixed); multi-batch opening fills for the same conId weren't accumulating into `position_legs.quantity` (fixed — quantity/entry_price now resync from IBKR's current truth on every pass, safe since IBKR always reports the current total, never an increment).

**Operational**: worker deploys are manual to the VPS (`npm run deploy:worker`), not automatic from a GitHub push — see [[project_worker_runs_on_vps]]. A dedicated every-10-min SSH health-check (forced-command key) verifies the worker's systemd unit is active and restarts it if not, verified against a real forced outage 2026-08-25.

**Rolling a covered call's short leg was silently double-counting the stock, found and fixed 2026-09-10.** A real AMAT and SPCX roll each left the *old* position's stock leg open forever: `runReconciliationPass`'s closing loop only closes a leg whose `ibkr_contract_id` is no longer held by IBKR, but a roll only replaces the *option* conId — the underlying shares never leave, so the stock leg's conId stays held and the leg is never flagged for closing. Meanwhile `upsertSplitCoveredCallPosition` saw the new call's never-before-seen conId, found no existing position for it, and created a brand-new position with a brand-new stock leg for the same physical shares — a permanent duplicate. Surfaced via the hourly reconciliation drift alert ("local total quantity=X but IBKR reports X/2", both off by exactly 2×). **Fixed**: `upsertSplitCoveredCallPosition` now looks for an orphaned stock leg (same conId, sibling option leg no longer IBKR-held) before creating a new position, and reassigns it instead of duplicating. The 2 already-corrupted AMAT/SPCX legs closed via a one-off script (`scripts/closeOrphanedRolledStockLegs.ts`, zero P&L — shares were never sold, just re-tagged by the roll), confirmed via direct prod query (`close_reason='closed_via_app'`, exit_price=entry_price, quantities now match IBKR exactly). Code fix committed and pushed same day, but the VPS worker itself wasn't actually redeployed until checked and caught the next session, 2026-09-10 19:57 UTC (`npm run deploy:worker`) — a GitHub push alone does **not** update it ([[project_worker_runs_on_vps]]), so it had been running the old buggy code the whole time despite PROGRESS.md briefly claiming otherwise. Confirmed via a prod-wide query that no other symbol has this duplicate-leg pattern. First reconciliation pass after the real deploy came back clean.

**Two more real bugs found and fixed 2026-08-27**: (1) an option leg that expired (or was assigned) with no matching `trades` row — the normal case for expiration, since IBKR never generates an execution for it — got `exit_price = null` instead of `0`, silently dropping it out of `realizedPnl`'s `SUM(... WHERE exit_price IS NOT NULL)` and reporting $0 P&L for every expired short option instead of the full premium kept; fixed in `reconcilePositionsFromIbkr`, backfilled two already-affected MU legs in prod, and added a Telegram + in-app toast notification (`notifyPositionExpired`, shared `formatPositionExpiredMessage`) for future expiry/assignment closes. (2) confirming an order tied to a trade alert flipped `trade_alerts.status` to `approved` with nothing ever reverting it if the order was then cancelled/rejected without filling — the alert stayed permanently unusable, breaking "cancel and resubmit with a different quantity." Fixed: both the local-cancel path and the worker's terminal-order-status path now revert the alert back to `pending` (`revertSourceAlertToPending.ts`).

Full IBKR trading-book/depth visibility, full option chain w/ all bid-ask, richer trade tab, and a new upcoming-events tab. Broken into independently-decidable pieces:
- [ ] Order confirmation needs real-time prices and the live option chain (Juan) — real-time subscription blocker resolved 2026-09-14; unblocked, just not built yet.
- [ ] Explore alternative IBKR order types for better fill price (marketable limit, midpoint/peg, adaptive algos) — not scoped yet.
- [ ] Click-to-open per-symbol depth-of-book — investigated 2026-08-25, actually blocked: `SMART` routing doesn't support depth at all (error 10092), and a real exchange requires its own paid depth subscription (error 10089, ~$1.50–$25/mo/exchange). Revisit once Juan confirms a depth subscription.
- [ ] Continuous real-time streaming on Screener/Positions (vs. today's daily-cached-snapshot design) — real architecture change; real-time subscription blocker resolved 2026-09-14, unblocked, just not scoped/built yet.
- [ ] Market data line limit / cost to raise — default min 100 lines; Quote Booster packs $30/mo per +100 L1 lines, max 10 packs (~$300/mo cap). Juan should confirm exact current pricing in his own account.
- [ ] ETF constituents (e.g. SMH holdings) — no IBKR API for this either. Needs a separate reference-data vendor, not started.

## Strategy domain notes (from Juan Dominguez, non-developer trading-domain user, via WhatsApp 2026-08-12)
Captured for the trade-alert-generation algorithm design — don't lose it.
- **Trade timing**: avoid executing right at market open; wait ~30 min. Anecdotal: Nasdaq direction after the first 30-min candle often predicts the day's trend (unverified). A sharp opening drop may mean CSP premiums pay more that day, and vice versa for calls.
- **Covered calls**: sell against anything held, always, ideally bought on a dip first. Generally *want* assignment (premium + appreciation to strike = max profit) — varies per ticker based on comfort holding long-term if not assigned. Higher/further strike = less premium now but keeps the stock for continued call-selling.
- **Cash-secured puts**: assignment usually *not* wanted. Worth checking moving averages/trend as a screening filter. Worst-case defense if assigned and stock drops: sell covered calls against the assigned shares to work back to breakeven.
- **Rolling**: when a short option has decayed to near-worthless, often worth buying it back even at a small cost to free up collateral, then selling further out for fresh premium.
- **Scenario comparison**: when choosing between candidate strikes, want P&L outcomes compared side by side under a price scenario — satisfied today by Trade Alerts' payoff-summary cards; a full interactive chart per candidate is deferred.
- **Overall framing**: covered calls are comparatively lower-risk; CSPs need more scrutiny since assignment means acquiring stock at strike. System objective: maximize premium income plus the "wheel" cycle (assigned → redeploy cash → repeat). No schema implication — operational philosophy executed via Screener/Trade Alerts.

## Decisions made
- Stack: Node.js backend, React frontend, PostgreSQL via Heroku Postgres Essential-1, Heroku hosting.
- UI template: Tabler (`@tabler/core` + `@tabler/icons-react`). Charting: ApexCharts (aggregate) + TradingView's lightweight-charts (OHLC/candlestick, our own component ported from menaris-admin-app's `LwChart.js`). Payoff diagrams are custom computed line/area series.
- Two users, same access level, no per-role permissions.
- Personalization (table column visibility, UI prefs) stored client-side in localStorage only.
- Strategies implemented in code, not via UI; schema/platform supports multiple strategies running in parallel. v1 scope: covered calls + cash-secured puts, both from day one.
- Historical options data / deep backtesting: shelved — 10 years of full-detail data doesn't fit a 10GB Postgres budget, most vendors' ToS block bulk export. Lightweight forward-simulation (self-accumulated data) planned instead.
- All market data (screening + pricing) sourced directly from IBKR, no third-party vendor. Realized/unrealized P&L sourced live from IBKR directly, not computed from our own records.
- **IBKR connectivity: dedicated Hetzner VPS** — Heroku dynos restart ~daily with an ephemeral filesystem (wrong fit for a stable Gateway needing to avoid repeated 2FA), the user's Mac can't guarantee market-hours uptime. `gnzsnz/ib-gateway-docker` + `@stoqey/ib`. Heroku-to-VPS bridge is an SSH tunnel with a dedicated, restricted key (`permitopen`+`command=` clause, `restrict` alone does not block exec-channel command execution). Two separate Gateway containers (`ib-gateway-paper`/`ib-gateway-live`) for independent restart schedules and crash isolation.
  - VPS live since 2026-08-13 at `142.132.185.128`. IBKR does not support a second/secondary user on a paper account — multi-user needs the live account. Juan owns his own IBKR credentials end-to-end.
- Domain: ioriore.com, `app.`/`api.` subdomain split via Cloudflare + Heroku ACM.
- Screener: daily cached snapshot (`market_data_snapshots`), not live-per-view IBKR queries. Made strategy-agnostic 2026-08-25 — `strategy_key` dropped from `shortlist_entries` (a shortlisted ticker is now scanned by every strategy's trade-alert job), after discovering CSP alerts never fired because no ticker had ever been shortlisted under that strategy.
- Shared `Spinner` component standardized for every non-immediate operation. See [[feedback_ui_loading_and_modal_dismiss_conventions]].
- Database schema (conceptual, finalized) — see below. Legs-based positions so future multi-leg strategies don't need a rewrite.
- Ticker Detail modal scoped around live pricing/chart/option chain (not fundamentals), platform-wide shared modal for every ticker symbol in the app.
- Option chain scope: strategy-relevant window (near-the-money strikes across nearby expiries in the DTE band), not the full chain — stays under IBKR's ~100 concurrent-market-data-line limit. Widened 2026-08-20 to include weeklies.
- Authentication: password-based, Argon2id, `express-session`+`connect-pg-simple` (Postgres, not Redis). No self-service password reset — CLI script only.
- **IV Rank formula** (approved 2026-08-21): `(current IV − min IV) / (max IV − min IV) × 100` over up to a trailing 252-trading-day window. Shown with the actual window size (e.g. "IV Rank (45d)") rather than hidden until a full year accumulates. Requires ≥2 distinct-IV points and a non-zero range, else null.
- **Yield/ranking formula** (approved): `(premium/capitalAtRisk)×(365/dte)`, annualized. See "Currently open" above for the follow-up discussion on what it doesn't account for.
- **Roll trigger thresholds** (approved 2026-08-21): close/roll when the leg has decayed to ≤50% of credit collected (tastytrade's "50% rule") OR DTE drops to ≤21, whichever first. No delta-based "avoid assignment" trigger — no rigorous backing found and it cuts against covered calls generally *wanting* assignment.
- **Roll IV-rank gate + net-credit floor** (approved 2026-08-31, built same day — `src/ibkr/generateRollCandidates.ts`, `src/lib/rollEconomics.ts`). Two refinements to the roll trigger above: (1) the 21-DTE trigger is a gamma/pin-risk control and fires unconditionally; the 50%-decay trigger is a discretionary profit-take and is now gated on the ticker's IV Rank ≥ 30 (top ~70% of its own 252-day range) — rolling early into a low-IV, cheap-premium environment isn't worth it. A ticker without enough IV history (`ivRank` null) is not gated, same "absence of data isn't evidence against" convention as `calendarUnverified`. (2) A roll is only ever suggested when `replacement.premium − closeLeg.currentPrice` (net credit) exceeds a **measured, not guessed**, minimum: 2× the account's own trailing average per-contract option commission (from `trades.commission`, IBKR's real `CommissionReport` — never a hardcoded published rate) + half the bid/ask spread on both the closing and replacement legs (read directly off the live quote, not assumed). Below 5 historical option fills, the commission component is $0 (no data, no penalty) rather than substituted with a guessed rate. If no candidate in the strategy's delta/DTE band clears this floor, no roll is suggested at all — Juan's explicit call: that's an acceptable outcome, not an error; he can hold to expiry/assignment or act manually. Deliberately does **not** attempt to guarantee a roll beats *not* rolling over the position's remaining life — that depends on future price/IV paths no formula can know; see the backlog below for that as a separate, explicitly probabilistic, low-priority idea. `RollSuggestion`/`RollStructure`/rationale text/Telegram formatting all surface `netCredit`/`requiredMinimumCredit`; refresh re-validates both and flags drift (`stillTriggered`/`stillNetCredit`) the same way. Both repos typecheck clean; not yet live-tested against a real triggered roll (no pending roll alert this session).
- **Allocation/exposure formula** (approved 2026-08-25): every % is against total account net liquidation value (positions + cash), not sum-of-positions, with an explicit "Unallocated" cash row.

## Database schema (conceptual — matches what's live)
- **users** — `id, email, display_name, password_hash, created_at`. Argon2id hash. No reset flow — CLI script only.
- **tickers** — `id, symbol, company_name, sector, ibkr_contract_id, created_at`.
- **shortlist_entries** — `id, ticker_id, added_by_user_id, added_at, removed_at, notes`. `removed_at` soft-deletes. (Name predates the strategy-agnostic decision above.)
- **positions** — `id, strategy_key, ticker_id, status (open/closed), ibkr_account_id, opened_at, closed_at, notes`. Strategy-agnostic shell.
- **position_legs** — `id, position_id, leg_type (stock/option), side (long/short), quantity, option_type, strike_price, expiry_date, multiplier, ibkr_contract_id, entry_price, entry_at, exit_price, exit_at`. `multiplier` isn't always 100 — see [[project_position_leg_multiplier_vs_quantity]].
- **trades** — `id, position_leg_id, ibkr_order_id, ibkr_exec_id (unique), side, quantity, price, commission, realized_pnl, executed_at, raw_ibkr_payload (jsonb)`. Execution blotter.
- **order_requests** — the web-to-worker order queue (see Order execution platform above). `payload` jsonb: `{symbol, strategyKey, legs: [{role, action, symbol, quantity, unitPrice, strike?, expiry?, right?, ibkrContractId?, positionLegId?}]}`.
- **trade_alerts** — `id, strategy_key, ticker_id, suggested_structure (jsonb), rationale, status (pending/approved/rejected/modified/expired), reviewed_by_user_id, reviewed_at, resulting_position_id`.
- **account_pnl_snapshots** — `id, snapshot_date, daily_pnl, realized_pnl, unrealized_pnl, net_liquidation_value, net_cash_flow, captured_at`. `daily_pnl` = net-liq delta minus `net_cash_flow`.
- **position_pnl_snapshots** — `id, position_id, snapshot_date, realized_pnl, unrealized_pnl, market_value, captured_at`. One row/day per open position.
- **daily_price_bars** — `id, ticker_id, trading_date, open_price, high_price, low_price, close_price, volume, captured_at`. Backs 1Y/5Y/All chart ranges (5Y/All resampled weekly in SQL) and Price Performance.
- **intraday_price_bars** — `id, ticker_id, bar_size, bar_time, ...OHLCV`, `(ticker_id, bar_size, bar_time)` unique. Backs 1D/5D/1M/3M/6M chart ranges. No split-adjustment (unadjusted `TRADES` bars) — a real stock split needs a manual `DELETE` on that ticker's cached rows.
- **option_chain_params** — one row per ticker: expirations/strikes arrays + `fetched_at`. 24h refresh.
- **option_chain_strike_checks** — `(ticker_id, expiry, strike)` unique, `exists` boolean + `checked_at`. `exists=true` cached forever; `exists=false` gets a 24h re-check window (an exchange can add a new strike after a large move).
- **market_data_snapshots** — `id, ticker_id, snapshot_date, implied_volatility, avg_option_volume, captured_at`. `avg_option_volume` was null under delayed data; populates under real-time (live since 2026-08-31, see Open decisions).
- **job_runs** — `id, job_name, started_at, finished_at, status (running/success/failure), error_message, details (jsonb)`. Backs System Health + Telegram alerts + Watchdog.
- **strategy_settings** — `id, strategy_key, delta_target_min/max, dte_target_min/max, max_position_pct_of_portfolio, max_aggregate_collateral_pct, max_concentration_per_ticker_pct, max_concentration_per_sector_pct, min_cash_reserve_pct, updated_at`.
- **genosuke_chat_messages**, **genosuke_preferences** — Genosuke's rolling 24h chat history and durable cross-conversation preferences (see Built section).

## Built
Everything below is real, working code, verified end-to-end (migrations against real databases, live HTTP/Playwright testing, real IBKR round-trips where applicable). Full debugging narratives condensed here — see git history/commit messages for the date mentioned if the full story is ever needed again.

**Pulse feed timestamps (2026-09-21, front end, trial):** Trades and Latest Events show `Xd ago` (via `formatFeedTime` in formatters.ts) for rows a full day or older; under 24h keeps HH:MM:SS. Labels are computed at row creation, so they don't roll over live. Checked in the DOM only (1d–9d labels render); no screenshot, no mobile check yet. A hover tooltip with the full date is an open option.

**Platform / infra**
- Both apps auto-deploy from GitHub → Heroku on push to `main`; Postgres Essential-1, migrations run via a `release:` Procfile phase.
- Auth (Argon2id + `express-session`/`connect-pg-simple`), `scripts/manage-user.ts`, global crash handlers (`installCrashHandlers.ts` — log + Telegram alert + `process.exit(1)`, verified against real forced crashes), global Express error handler.
- IBKR client (`src/ibkr/`) — SSH-tunneled `@stoqey/ib` connection, explicit-required env vars, verified via real round-trips. Recurring gotchas fixed across the build: missing `clientId`/connect timeout could hang a second concurrent connection forever ([[project_ibkr_connection_clientid_timeout]]); `once()` listeners could orphan other concurrent id-scoped listeners ([[project_eventemitter_once_concurrent_listeners]]); ambiguous `reqContractDetails` calls get throttled, fully-qualified ones don't ([[project_ibkr_option_strikes_per_expiry]]); an unhandled `'error'` on the SSH-forwarded pipe stream crashed the whole process on any transient network blip (fixed 2026-08-27, `ibkrGatewayTunnel.ts`); a stalled `sshClient.connect()` inside the same file had no timeout and could freeze the worker's reconnect loop forever with no further logs (separate bug, also fixed 2026-08-27 — see the "Order execution platform" section for the 8-hour outage this caused).
- **Caching layer, built 2026-08-27 after a full IBKR call-site inventory** to cut throttling risk: contract details (conId/company/sector) cached on the `tickers` row, effectively-immutable; option chain params + per-strike existence checks cached (24h refresh, see schema above) — both proven via sentinel-poisoning tests (overwrote a cached value, confirmed the app actually reads the poisoned value rather than a fresh live one, then restored it). Chart bars (daily + new intraday table) cache-then-delta-fetch instead of re-pulling full history on every range switch. Genuinely live-vs-live-only data (positions, account cash/exposure, streaming quotes) deliberately left uncached.
- `sync-dev-db` script pulls prod DB to local dev, preserves local users across the sync. See [[project_sync_dev_db_script]].

**Frontend shell** — React 19 + Vite + Tabler + ApexCharts + lightweight-charts. Session-aware routing, all 10 screens (8 at the time of this entry), column-visibility gear-icon `DataTable` (localStorage-persisted), dark/light theme toggle, mobile-verified at 390×844 throughout. Shared `formatters.ts`/`payoff.ts` libraries. **Full-app theme/contrast audit (2026-09-01)** — triggered by a real bug: `PositionCard.tsx`'s ApexChart payoff-annotation labels (Breakeven/Current lines) had no theme-aware `label.style.color`/`background`, so they fell through to ApexCharts' own unthemed default rather than the app's `annotationColorsByTheme` map, which only covered the line's `borderColor` — fixed by setting both explicitly per theme. Prompted a 4-way parallel audit of all 9 pages + shared chrome (light/dark × desktop/mobile + modals): no other contrast bugs found — `PositionCard.tsx` was the only place in the app using ApexChart `annotations` at all, and the global `.badge.bg-*-lt` contrast override in `theme.css:492-551` (`!important`, both themes identical) already protects every other badge in the app. Also fixed in the same pass: a real React key-prop bug on `/trade-alerts` (`rollAlerts.map()` returned a shorthand `<>` fragment instead of `<Fragment key={alert.id}>`, so the inner `<tr key=...>` never satisfied React's requirement — the map callback's returned element itself needs the key); removed several dead `text-dark` classes on `-lt` badges across `formatters.ts`/`positionPnl.ts`/`RiskLimitsPage.tsx`/`CalendarEventsPage.tsx`/`TickerDetailModal.tsx` (the CSS override forces white text regardless, so `text-dark` was always a no-op, just misleading in source); deleted unused dead file `CandlestickChart.tsx`.

**Screener** — single cross-strategy monitored-ticker list (`LEFT JOIN LATERAL` on latest snapshot), IV Rank column (formula above), search-as-you-type (`reqMatchingSymbols`, 400ms debounce). Ad-hoc "+ New Position"/"Trade" entry point removed 2026-08-27 per Marcelo's call — new positions now only originate from a Trade Alert or Genosuke.

**Ticker Detail modal** — platform-wide shared modal (SSE-streamed), live pricing/chart/strategy-relevant option chain with Greeks. Redesigned 2026-08-26 to fold Trade Alerts into the modal itself (pending alerts table above the chain, matched strikes get a clickable order-setup panel), prices switched to genuinely continuous live streaming (not polled/throttled). Real IBKR errors now surface for this screen's calls instead of generic timeouts. Several rounds of live-testing polish on 2026-08-27 (spot-price marker row, `formatCurrencyTrimmed` for whole-dollar strikes, stale-price-freeze bug on IBKR's `-1` sentinel tick fixed across all pricing paths, Order Setup preview panel gained Max Gain/Max Loss/Breakeven). **Collapsible sections, 2026-09-08** — Positions/History/Trade Alerts/Option Chain/Technicals now use the same `CollapsibleCard` pattern as the Dashboard, each remembering its open/closed state in localStorage; Price Chart and IV History (which already render their own `.card` chrome) instead drive their existing headers via a new shared `useCollapsibleCard` hook rather than nesting a second card. "Show/Hide History" button replaced by History defaulting closed. Option Chain and Positions force themselves open when something scrolls to them from elsewhere (alert click, "Sell Call", multi-position navigation) even if the user had them collapsed. Two bugs found and fixed during this work: a "skip the first effect run" ref trick for the force-open signal broke under this app's React StrictMode (double-invoked mount effects reopened a deliberately-collapsed card on every mount) — replaced with a value-comparison against the previous signal instead of a mount-order assumption; `PositionCard` renders its own full `.card`, so wrapping it in `CollapsibleCard` produced a visibly nested card — fixed by demoting `PositionCard`'s own wrapper to a plain unbordered container (it's now only ever rendered inside this one collapsible card).

**Order Review panel** (`OrderReviewPanel.tsx`, shared across New Position/Roll/Close) — DTE, capital-at-risk/EXP%, max gain/loss/breakeven, live bid/ask/spread/IV/Greeks via a genuinely live SSE quote stream. Opening orders get a live delta-compliance check (`checkDeltaCompliance` against `strategy_settings`) that disables Confirm until a compliant quote arrives — fails closed. Also shows live cash-sufficiency (Available Cash / Cash Required / Cash After Trade), netting cash already committed to open CSPs.

**Live-value flash indicator** (`useFlashOnChange.ts`, 2026-09-01) — every genuinely SSE-ticking numeric value across the platform briefly flashes amber (`.flash-changed`, 1200ms hold-then-fade) when its live quote stream ticks it to a new number: Order Review's bid/ask/spread/IV/Greeks, Roll Position's and Close Position's per-leg live quote lines, and — the biggest surface — Ticker Detail's spot price/bid/ask/day-range/volume, the whole option chain table (bid/ask/delta/yield, calls and puts), and the embedded Trade Alerts table's live delta/premium/yield. Neutral color deliberately, not directional green/up red/down — a changed IV/theta/vega isn't inherently good or bad. Two real bugs found live-testing against actual IBKR ticks: (1) the CSS `from(full color)→to(transparent)` fade with `ease-out` decayed almost all its opacity in the opening moments, invisible on a small text cell — fixed by holding full color through the first 35% of the animation; (2) Ticker Detail's option chain/spot price were wrongly assumed one-shot-per-open (matching a similar-looking but genuinely one-shot stream elsewhere) — `streamTickerDetail.ts` actually keeps pushing live ticks for as long as the modal stays open, confirmed correct via a JS-side interval poll capturing real ticks. Every live SSE consumer in the app got audited (grepped every `EventSource`/stream-opener call site) — this covers all of them; Dashboard/Positions/Price Performance/etc. load once per visit and have nothing to flash. **Scoped to the number, not the cell (2026-09-09)** — the animation's `box-shadow: inset 0 0 0 9999px` fills whatever box it's applied to, and it had been applied to the whole `<td>` on the option chain and Trade Alerts tables, so a bid/ask/delta/yield tick visibly flashed the entire (sometimes background-tinted) cell. Moved the `flash-changed` class onto a `<span>`/badge wrapping just the number in both `OptionSideCells` and `TradeAlertTableRow` — the highlight now outlines only the digits.

**Position-opened notification, fixing a stale-UI race after a fill (2026-09-09).** Found live on a real SPCX cash-secured put: the Ticker Detail modal's Positions section stayed empty right after the order filled, only showing the new position once the modal was closed and reopened. Root cause: `OrderReviewPanel` calls its `onFilled` callback the instant `order_requests.status` flips to `"filled"`, but the actual `positions`/`position_legs` rows aren't created until `reconcilePositionsFromIbkr` runs — a separate async pass triggered by the IBKR `execDetails` event, confirmed on prod to land ~2.6s later. `onFilled` fired a `loadPositions()` too early, got an empty result, and nothing re-fetched afterward — no notification existed for "reconciliation just created a new position," unlike the existing `position_closed` one. Fixed by adding a `position_opened` case to the shared `AppNotification` type (`src/lib/notificationChannel.ts` + app's `api/notifications.ts`) and publishing it from every place `ibkrGatewayWorker.ts`'s reconciliation actually inserts a new `positions` row (new CSP/covered-call/unstructured, plus the two merged-position split-off paths). `TickerDetailModal` now refetches on a matching-symbol event; `PositionsPage` refetches on any such event (not symbol-scoped). Verified live against the dev DB/app: a manual `pg_notify` of `position_opened` triggered an immediate positions re-fetch on both screens, and a non-matching symbol correctly produced no extra fetch on the modal. `PositionsPage` has the same kind of gap for `position_closed` (a position closing elsewhere while the page is open won't drop off the list without a manual refresh) — flagged, not yet fixed, no ticket for it.

**Ticker Detail price bar — Ex-Div/Earnings dates, compact volume (2026-09-09).** Bid/Ask dropped from the bar (redundant with the option chain's own bid/ask columns); Volume now goes through a new `formatCompactNumber` helper (`formatters.ts`) — 3-significant-digit k/M formatting (1234 → "1.23k", 1234567 → "1.23M"), with a rounding-carry guard so a value like 999,500 promotes to the next unit instead of printing "1000k". Added next Ex-Dividend/Earnings dates, sourced from the existing `ticker_calendar_events` table (already fed nightly for shortlisted/open-position tickers by `job:daily-calendar-capture`) via a new `GET /calendar-events/next/:symbol` endpoint; a ticker with no captured rows yet (this modal opens for any ticker platform-wide, not just shortlisted ones) triggers `captureTickerCalendarEvents` on the spot — same on-demand TradingView fetch the shortlist-add flow already uses — rather than showing a permanent blank. Verified live for both a non-dividend-payer (PLTR, correctly shows "—" for Ex-Div) and a dividend payer (CSCO, resolved real Oct/Nov 2026 dates on first open). Day range display tightened to `$168.61-171.6` (single `$`, no spaces around the dash) and the bar's labels (Day range/Volume/Ex-Div/Earnings) bolded, both per Marcelo's request.

**Ticker Detail modal survives a page refresh (2026-09-09).** The open symbol now lives in a `?ticker=SYMBOL` URL query param (new `useTickerDetailSymbol` hook, `app/src/hooks/`) instead of local `useState`, on all 7 pages that open this modal (Screener, Trade Alerts, Positions, Calendar, Dashboard, Trade Blotter, Risk & Limits) — reloading the browser with the modal open now reopens it on the same ticker instead of losing it, and closing the modal clears the param again (`replace: true` throughout, so opening/closing never spams browser history). Positions and Dashboard also pass a one-shot `focusPositionId` ("scroll to this position") alongside the symbol — deliberately kept in plain local state, not the URL, per Marcelo's call: not worth the extra plumbing to survive a refresh.

**Positions** — legs-based CRUD, live per-leg Greeks, live unrealized P&L (`GET /positions/pnl`), EXP$/EXP% exposure columns. Close accepts partial-quantity downsizing (option-leg-driven, stock always derived). No manual position-creation entry point (see Screener above). **FROZEN pricing + progressive streaming, 2026-09-11.** Marcelo noticed a position (AMAT) showing no MV/P&L at all — root cause: `fetchLivePrices.ts`'s batch price fetch (backing both `GET /positions/pnl` and the nightly P&L snapshot job) requested REALTIME market data, which for a thinly-traded option leg can simply never produce a completing tick within the request window, nulling the whole position (and, in the snapshot job, skipping writing a row for it entirely). Switched to FROZEN (same fix already applied to `streamLivePrices.ts` for the SSE screens, approved 2026-09-09) — returns the last known price immediately instead of gating on a live trade occurring. Verified directly against the API: AMAT's leg resolved and the full 8-position/11-leg batch completed in ~2.2s. Separately, found the Positions table's P&L/Greeks columns were still calling the old one-shot blocking endpoints (`fetchUnrealizedPnl`/`fetchGreeks`) even though the already-built, already-approved streaming siblings (`GET /positions/pnl/stream`, `GET /positions/greeks/stream` — FROZEN-then-live, same mechanics as Ticker Detail) existed and were simply unused by this screen. Rewired `PositionsPage.tsx` to `openUnrealizedPnlStream`/`openGreeksStream` (new wrappers in `api/positions.ts`, `EventSource`-based, same shape as `openNotificationStream`) — frontend-only change, no backend work needed. Verified both streams tick progressively: P&L and all four Greeks (delta/gamma/vega/theta) start from a stale snapshot fallback and upgrade to live values within ~1s, visibly updating in place rather than the whole table blocking then painting at once. `TickerDetailModal.tsx` still uses the plain one-shot `fetchGreeks`/`fetchUnrealizedPnl` — out of scope for this pass, not yet switched.

**DTE off-by-one across the app, fixed 2026-09-11.** Marcelo spotted a same-day (0DTE) expiry showing "-1 DTE" in the Ticker Detail Position card. Root cause: `daysToExpiry()` (`formatters.ts`) defaults its "today" argument to raw `new Date()` (current instant) when omitted, diffed against the expiry parsed as UTC midnight — for any same-day expiry, that diff is negative for essentially the whole US trading day, rounding to -1. The 2026-09-09 fix (passing `todayInEasternIso()` explicitly) was only ever applied to the two DTE-only columns (Positions table, Calendar); four more call sites going through the `formatExpiryWithDte` wrapper with `asOf` omitted were never touched: `PositionCard.tsx`'s leg table (the reported bug), `OrderReviewPanel.tsx` (both the leg-description label and the live Ann. Yield calc — the latter had been silently computing yield off `-1`/NaN days for 0DTE positions since the feature shipped), and `ClosePositionModal.tsx` (both the leg label and the Contract summary panel). All four now pass `todayInEasternIso()`. `formatExpiryWithDte` still has no 0/negative special-casing of its own (unlike `formatDaysToExpiry`, used only by the two DTE-only columns) — a future off-by-one would again render as a raw negative number; left as-is, flagged for whoever touches this next.

**Ticker Detail Position card parity + streaming fix, 2026-09-11.** Two more things Marcelo caught live-testing the DTE fix: (1) the card's "Premium P&L: —" spinner span could spin forever — root cause was the same one-shot-fetch gap noted above (`fetchGreeks`/`fetchUnrealizedPnl` in `TickerDetailModal.tsx`), now actually fixed by converting it to `openGreeksStream`/`openUnrealizedPnlStream` (same pattern as PositionsPage, 2026-09-11 above) via two new `[positions]`-keyed effects, re-opening whenever the symbol's position list changes; (2) the card was missing several columns/stats the Positions table shows for the same position — added a Gamma column to the per-leg table (alongside the existing Delta) and EXP $/EXP %/MV/Opened as summary stats next to the existing P&L/Premium P&L/Stock P&L badges (EXP % needed `totalAccountValue`, newly fetched in `TickerDetailModal.tsx` the same way PositionsPage already does). Also, unprompted but noticed in the same screenshot: the Roll button (both the position-level one on Positions and the leg-level "Roll Alert" one in this card) switched from solid `btn-warning` to `btn-outline-warning` to match Close's outline-then-fill-on-hover style, keeping the warning color.

**Flash-on-change extended to the Positions table, 2026-09-11.** Same `useFlashOnChange`/`.flash-changed` mechanism already used in Ticker Detail's quote rows, now on the Positions table's live-streamed numbers (P&L $/%, Premium/Stock P&L, MV, Delta, Gamma). `DataTable.tsx`'s columns call `render(row)` as a plain function per cell (not a component), so calling the hook directly there would violate the Rules of Hooks once the row count changes between renders — packaged as a new reusable `<FlashingNumber value precision?>` component (`components/FlashingNumber.tsx`) instead, which is a real component instance per cell and can safely hold its own hook state. Scoped to the number span only, not the `<td>`, per Marcelo's ask.

**Price Target / Close Trigger Notes / Notes removed entirely, 2026-09-11.** Marcelo's call — never used in practice (confirmed via direct query: 0 of 37 real positions had any of the three set, in both local dev and prod, before dropping anything). Removed the `positions.notes`/`price_target`/`close_trigger_notes` columns (migration `20260911000001_drop_notes_price_target_from_positions.ts`), the now-fully-dead `PATCH /positions/:id` route (it existed only to write these three fields — nothing else used it), the Genosuke `update_position_notes` tool (same route) and the dead `notes`/`priceTarget` params on `POST /positions/orders`'s `create_position` tool (already unused server-side — declared in the route's request-body type but never actually read), the "Price Target"/"Close Trigger Notes"/"Notes" form + Save button in `PositionCard.tsx`, and the Positions table's Notes column (whose only other job — a click-through to Ticker Detail — is already covered by the Symbol column).

**Trade Blotter** — real fills only (`trades`), merged with in-flight `order_requests` rows in one timeline with a live "IBKR State" column; relative-time labels; Cancel button on any `pending_confirmation` order. Mobile bug fixed 2026-09-05: a long unbounded IBKR error message wrapped into a ~193px-tall row (invisible on wide desktop tables, but showed as blank scrolled space on mobile where `table-responsive` clips the explanation off-screen) — now truncated to one line with a tooltip.

**Trade Alerts** — full pipeline: scheduled scan/ranking (see Scheduled jobs) + review UI. Redesigned 2026-08-27 to one card per ticker (New Trade / Roll tables) instead of one per alert; per-alert Dismiss/Reject removed in favor of scan-supersedes-stale; per-ticker on-demand Refresh added; Review opens the Ticker Detail modal with the order panel already open. Roll alerts execute a real leg swap (`POST /positions/:id/roll`), not just a suggestion. **Scan toast fixed 2026-09-11**: Marcelo noticed the "Trade Alert Scan" toast always said "Cash-Secured Puts" even though Covered Calls are scanned too — the backend (`runTradeAlertGeneration.ts`) fires a `strategyStart` event for every configured strategy back-to-back before any per-ticker scanning begins (both strategies are actually evaluated together per ticker, not as separate sequential phases), so the toast's message only ever showed whichever event fired last. `BackgroundJobsContext.tsx` now accumulates every strategy label seen in the current run instead of showing just the latest one.

**Risk & Limits** — `strategy_settings` thresholds with field help tooltips; live exposure/concentration by ticker/sector against those thresholds (with an "over limit" badge); allocation breakdown by strategy/top-position/sector (see Decisions made formula).

**Dashboard** — aggregate P&L (day/week/month/year, calendar-based), realized/unrealized state, per-strategy breakdown (realized P&L sourced from `position_legs` directly so a closed position's gain isn't dropped), Allocation card, Cash card. Mobile bug fixed 2026-09-05 (the actual bug Marcelo reported — "Latest Events" rows appearing blank with big gaps when scrolling on mobile): a position with many rolled legs produced a leg-description cell that wrapped into a **3062px-tall row** — same root cause as the Trade Blotter fix below, only reproduced after syncing real prod data locally (dev DB had no events data to trigger it). Now clamped to 3 lines (`-webkit-line-clamp`) with a tooltip for the full text. Latest Events "expired worthless" status badge renamed to "Expired" 2026-09-08 (display label only — `close_reason` DB value stays `expired_worthless`).

**Price Performance** (8th screen, 2026-08-25) — 24/48/72hr/1-week/1-month price changes per shortlisted ticker. **Redesigned 2026-09-11**: the six daily/weekly/monthly Hi/Lo columns and "As Of" removed to make room for MACD Trend and MA Trend columns (reusing `computeMacd`/`buildTrendLabel`, the same functions Ticker Detail's Technicals panel and Trade Alerts already use — via `fetchCachedPriceBars`, a separate `GET /price-performance/trends` endpoint so a cold cache's live IBKR fallback doesn't block the main table load) plus IV %/IV Rank/IV %ile/Avg Vol (mirrors `shortlist.ts`'s `market_data_snapshots` join + `computeIvMetrics` call exactly, so these can't drift from the Screener's numbers). MACD Trend and MA Trend both styled with `badge-change-pos/neg/flat` (unified after Marcelo noticed the two looked inconsistent — MA Trend had been copied from Trade Alerts' `bg-*-lt` tint style, MACD Trend from Ticker Detail's solid-badge style). **Current price + all five % change columns now genuinely live-streamed** (`GET /price-performance/current-prices/stream`, `openPricePerformanceStream` in the frontend) instead of loaded once via `fetchLivePrices`/`fetchCurrentPrices` — reuses `streamLivePrices`'s FROZEN-then-REALTIME SSE mechanics, the same web-dyno-direct IBKR read path Positions' P&L/Greeks streams already use (not the worker's persistent trading connection, which is a separate constraint about order execution only). The % change columns recompute against the live streamed price rather than the last captured daily close specifically so they can't visually disagree with the Current cell's own red/green coloring (which already compared live price vs. last close) — the pre-existing static, close-vs-close figures from `GET /price-performance` are kept only as the pre-stream-connect fallback. Every live number (Current + all five badges) flashes via the existing `FlashingNumber`/`useFlashOnChange` mechanism, same as Positions. 24/48/72hr remain trading-day close deltas, not true rolling 24-hour windows; 1W/1M remain a rolling calendar-day window. **Two follow-up fixes, same day**: (1) Current's red/green was still wrong — it compared the live price to the last completed daily close, so it could show red while every % badge showed green (or vice versa) since those got fixed to compare against the live price in the change above. Marcelo's call: Current should be colored by its own tick-to-tick direction (green when the new price is higher than its own previous tick, red when lower), and should not recolor or flash at all when the price hasn't moved at the 2-decimal precision actually shown — new `useTickDirectionColor` hook + `TickColoredPrice` component (`app/src/hooks/`, `app/src/components/`), seeded on the very first tick by the last daily close (no prior tick to compare against yet), every tick after that compares to the previous one. (2) `GET /price-performance/trends` started intermittently blanking a handful of the 15 shortlisted tickers' MACD/MA Trend cells (different ones each load, confirmed via repeated direct curl probes — 2/15 failed one run, 0/15 the next) once it started running concurrently with the new `current-prices/stream` connection on every page load — firing 15 IBKR historical-data fetches in one `Promise.all` now bursts past IBKR's pacing under that extra concurrent load. Fixed by batching the fetch into groups of 4 (sequential batches, parallel within each) — confirmed clean (0/15 blank) across 3 repeated loads after the fix, previously intermittent before it.

**System Health** — reads `job_runs`, on-demand real IBKR handshake check, collapsible issue summary (not a raw JSON dump). A second render path (the plain-success case, no `problems` array) still dumped raw JSON output inline uncollapsed on nearly every row — same mobile tall-row bug as Dashboard/Trade Blotter above, fixed 2026-09-05 by collapsing it behind the same "View output" `<details>` disclosure already used for the `problems`-found case, plus truncating the failure-case error message.

**Update-available toast** (`NewVersionToast.tsx`, 2026-09-01) — polls `/` every 5 min (`cache: "no-store"`), regex-extracts the bundled `<script src>` hash from the returned `index.html`, and compares it to the hash captured on first load; a mismatch shows a top-right toast with a Reload button. Exists because SPAs never swap out already-loaded JS on their own — a long-open tab can silently run stale code after a deploy. Fixed same day: shipped with a leftover `useState(true) // TEMP: forced on for contrast check`, so the toast showed unconditionally on every load and never cleared (Reload just remounted the component back to forced-`true`) — corrected to `useState(false)`.

**Mobile UI audit, 2026-09-11.** Marcelo asked for an exhaustive mobile pass across all 10 screens. First attempt (measuring table `scrollWidth`/accessibility-tree only, no actual scrolling/clicking) over-reported: most tables flagged "CRITICAL — too wide" turned out to scroll cleanly with legible headers at every position once actually looked at. Caught directly by Marcelo screenshotting a real bug the first pass missed — Dashboard's Latest Events table had its Description/Value headers visually overlapping into garbled text on horizontal scroll, invisible to both methods used (scrollWidth proves overflow exists but not what it looks like; the accessibility tree reads DOM text nodes as individually correct even when their pixel positions collide). Redone with a visual-first method — scroll every overflowing table to 3+ positions and screenshot each, click every collapsible/tab/filter/modal, toggle theme, all before touching source — and delegated to a background agent per [[feedback_parallel_agents_share_one_playwright_session]] (single agent, no parallelism). Confirmed and fixed 5 real defects: (1) Dashboard Latest Events header/cell collision — `DashboardPage.tsx`'s Description `<col>` was `width: "auto"` under `tableLayout: "fixed"`, which collapses an auto column instead of sizing it to content; given an explicit `18rem` width. (2) `PositionCard.tsx`'s payoff-chart Breakeven/Current annotation labels overlapping when the two prices are close (seen on SPCX) — root cause turned out to be paint-order overlap between the two rotated SVG label boxes (confirmed via `getBoundingClientRect()` on the actual label elements, not guesswork), fixed with enough `offsetY` separation between them (4 vs 65) plus `grid.padding.top` for headroom. (3) Trade Alerts' mobile card-level Review/Roll buttons used `btn-sm`, a direct CLAUDE.md violation (`btn-sm` is for table rows/tight inline groups only) — the corresponding desktop table-row buttons correctly kept it, only the full-width mobile card versions were wrong. (4) System Health's "View output" JSON `<pre>` block had no width constraint, so expanding it silently pushed the row off-screen with no scroll cue — now wraps in place (`white-space: pre-wrap`, `overflow-x: auto`). (5) Calendar's economic-events Actual/Forecast/Previous columns rendered raw unformatted strings ("0.200000") instead of going through a shared formatter like the adjacent Amount column — now use `formatNumber(value, 2)`. All verified live post-fix, typecheck clean.

**Mobile burger menu wouldn't close, fixed 2026-09-11.** Marcelo reported the mobile nav's burger menu opened fine but wouldn't close — not via the X, not by tapping a nav link. Root cause: Vite's dev server was pre-bundling `@tabler/core`'s Bootstrap JS twice, as two independent live module instances each running their own copy of Bootstrap's `Collapse` component with its own global `document` click listener for `data-bs-toggle="collapse"`. `main.tsx` imports the deep path (`@tabler/core/dist/js/tabler.esm.min.js`); `HelpTooltip.tsx` separately imported the bare `"@tabler/core"` specifier, which resolves via that package's `"module"` field to a *different* file (`dist/js/tabler.esm.js`) — Vite dedupes pre-bundled deps by resolved file path, not by package, so this produced two live copies. On every click both instances' handlers fired: one correctly started closing the menu, the other read the DOM immediately after (already mutated by the first), decided the menu looked closed, and reopened it — confirmed by monkey-patching `classList.add/remove` and capturing call stacks, which showed the two calls originating from two differently-hashed `.vite/deps` bundle files. Fixed by pointing `HelpTooltip.tsx`'s import at the same deep path as `main.tsx` (one shared Bootstrap instance app-wide); `types/tabler-core.d.ts` updated to match. Separately, and unrelated to the bug, Bootstrap's `Collapse` never auto-closes on a nav-link click by default — added an explicit handler in `AppLayout.tsx` that calls `Collapse.getOrCreateInstance(menu).hide()` when a nav item is clicked while the mobile menu is open. Verified on both mobile (390×844) and desktop (1440×900) via Playwright, plus tooltip behavior (the other consumer of the now-deduped import) confirmed still working. The stale comments in `ColumnVisibilityPopover.tsx`/`CollapsibleCard.tsx` claiming "no Bootstrap JS is loaded in this app" are incorrect — it was loaded (twice, until this fix); left as-is since those components' hand-rolled approach still works fine and isn't part of this bug.

**Genosuke — conversational Telegram bot** (built 2026-08-21/22, ported/adapted from menaris-admin-api's "Jack"). Webhook-based (switched from long-polling 2026-08-24, `POST /genosuke/webhook`), chat-ID allowlist gate, tool-calling loop against the app's own REST endpoints as an authenticated service user (not reimplemented route logic — see [[project_internal_api_client_pattern]]). Three tool tiers: read-only (10, no confirm), low-stakes write (5, immediate), financial-write (5, gated behind a real Telegram inline Yes/Cancel keyboard, in-memory `PendingConfirmation` with a 10-min TTL). Live in prod since 2026-08-24. Per-chat history moved off an in-memory `Map` (lost on every deploy) to a DB-backed `genosuke_chat_messages` table with a genuinely rolling 24h window (2026-08-27); `genosuke_preferences` table + `save_preference`/`forget_preference` tools let Marce/Juan set durable cross-conversation instructions — Genosuke only offers to remember on its own initiative for durable behavioral instructions, never one-off requests.

### Price-fetch fixes (2026-09-18, empirical probe of every market-data mode on the 4-14 open prod legs)
- **Bug fixed:** FROZEN mode delivers `last` (tick 4/68) AND `close` (9/75); `close` is the PRIOR session's close. `fetchLivePrices`/`streamLivePrices` kept only the latest tick, so `close` overwrote `last` and stocks were priced at yesterday's close (AAOI 98.06 vs last 104.90). Fixed by using ONLY `last` (tick 4/68); the prior-close fallback was then dropped entirely (decided 2026-09-19: a stale prior close is worse than showing nothing) — a contract with no `last` is null (Positions falls back to its snapshot, Exposure to entry_price). Affected Positions P&L, Exposure/Risk, Trade Alerts + Price Performance current price, and the covered-call stock limit-price auto-fill. Verified: stream/one-shot now return last. Since close is gone there is no prior-close flash to suppress. The SPCX call briefly showing 4.10 once (= its ask, a tick type the code can't accept) was NOT reproducible in 12 runs; cause unknown.
- **Daily P&L snapshot job** no longer uses `fetchLivePrices`: new `src/ibkr/fetchDailyClosingPrices.ts` — stocks from today's daily bar (`reqHistoricalData`, useRTH=0 = last incl. after-hours; bar date must equal the ET session date), options from IBKR's portfolio mark (`reqAccountUpdates`, rows with position != 0 only). Both end on real IBKR signals; only a 30s gateway-silent liveness guard. Decisions approved by Marcelo 2026-09-18 (mark vs last is a deliberate mixed definition for options).
- **Findings:** IBKR sends `tickSnapshotEnd` for stocks but NEVER for options in any mode -> no deterministic "done" for a one-shot option price; `reqHistoricalData` returns nothing for options (error 162); REALTIME gives options no price at all, FROZEN does. Standalone scripts that use the shared read connection collide with the running dev server's clientId 44 (looks like a 10s `fetchLivePrices`).
- **Consequence of dropping close:** the one-shot `fetchLivePrices` can no longer finish early for an option with no `last` (no completion signal exists for options), so a batch containing one costs the 6s ceiling (measured 14.6s vs 10.5s incl. connection). Only unused/stock-only one-shot paths remain (backend `/exposure` + `/positions/pnl` GETs, covered-call stock auto-fill), so it's parked until the ceiling is shrunk (see TODO below).
- **Still open (needs a design decision):** (1) stream still has a fixed 3s `frozenGraceMs` and one-shot still has the 6s ceiling — replacing them with "settled" signals gets stuck on an option that never gets a `last`; (2) Positions/Exposure one-shot vs stream-only for options; (3) midpoint valuation and labeling a prior-close fallback on screen (deferred, formula sign-off).
- **Dashboard / Pulse / Risk & Limits moved to the exposure stream (2026-09-19, app repo, uncommitted until asked):** `useExposureStream` hook over the existing `GET /risk-limits/exposure/stream`; Dashboard's Portfolio tiles are derived from the same reading (`portfolioFromExposure`) so each page opens ONE IBKR stream, not two. Removed the frontend one-shot `fetchExposure`/`fetchPortfolio` (backend one-shot routes left in place, now unused by the app). Tested with Playwright desktop + mobile (390px): final values tie out to hand-computed exposure (SPCX 29,725 / NBIS 22,225 / AAOI 10,490); first reading ~7s after page load. **Partial first reading fixed (Option A, approved 2026-09-19):** `streamLivePrices` passes `{ frozenPhaseComplete }` to its callback, and `streamPositionExposures` holds its first emit until every leg is priced or the frozen phase has ended (before this, unpriced legs fell back to `entry_price` and covered calls read 29,443 -> 29,725 in the first ~0.5s). Verified with `tmp/verifyExposureFirstEmit.ts`, 3 runs: first emit is already 29,724 / 32,715. Not tested: the stream-error-before-first-reading path (error message) and dark theme.
- **TODO when the market is open (Mon 9:30 ET+, decided 2026-09-19):** probe whether one FROZEN *streaming* request (snapshot=false, no phase switch) keeps ticking live during market hours. If yes, it replaces the two-phase FROZEN->REALTIME design and deletes the fixed 3s `frozenGraceMs` (and the close-only hold-back tied to it). Fallback if no: two IBKR connections per stream (frozen snapshot + REALTIME stream), which costs an extra handshake per stream. Why the 3s wait exists: `tmp/probeBackToBackFrozenAndRealtime.ts` (2026-09-19) showed the market-data type is applied when IBKR processes the request, so issuing FROZEN then REALTIME back to back on one connection turns the frozen snapshots into REALTIME (options then get no `last`, only a late close). After hours, FROZEN streaming already returns last/close/bid/ask for all legs. Then shrink the one-shot 6s ceiling to a failure-only guard (only stock-only callers remain once Dashboard/Pulse/Risk use streams).
- Probes: `tmp/probePriceModesForOpenPositions.ts`, `tmp/probeDailyJobPriceSources.ts`, `tmp/probeDailyBarUseRthZero.ts`, `tmp/verifyPriceFixes.ts`. Re-run at 9:35 ET to confirm behavior during market hours (all results so far are after-hours).

## Screen list (finalized, 10 screens + shared modals, all built)
General (cross-strategy): Dashboard, Positions, Trade Blotter, System Health, Risk & Limits, Screener, Price Performance, Calendar (added 2026-08-31).
Strategy-specific (tabs within the screen): Trade Alerts.
Standalone/chromeless (own tab, no sidebar/topbar): Iorio Pulse (added 2026-09-13) — real-time ops-room dashboard, see the entry at the top of this file.
Shared modal: Ticker Detail (live pricing/chart/option chain w/ Greeks, platform-wide — not yet showing position/strike/entry markers on the chart) — consolidated 2026-08-31 to also show the symbol's open positions (legs, payoff diagram, one card per open position; the price target/close-trigger fields were removed 2026-09-11) and closed-position history in one place; the separate Position Detail modal was folded in and no longer exists.

## Scheduled jobs (Heroku Scheduler) — 8 jobs (#8 `expiry_settlement_audit`, daily 10:00 PM UTC incl. weekends, added 2026-09-19 and in the watchdog's daily list; details in the 'Wheel-cycle engine' / 'Expiry-classification audit' entries above), all skip weekends except daily calendar capture (runs 7 days/week)
All 7 entries set to Basic dyno size in the Scheduler dashboard (manual, no CLI/API access to that setting).
1. **Daily price/IV capture** (`job:daily-market-data`, 9:00 PM UTC) — fixed and confirmed working 2026-08-28 (health check now probes `reqHistoricalData`); see "Currently open" above.
2. **Daily P&L snapshot** (`job:daily-pnl-snapshot`, 9:30 PM UTC) — `account_pnl_snapshots`+`position_pnl_snapshots` from IBKR.
3. **Option-chain capture → SVI surface fit → Trade Alert generation** (`job:option-chain-capture`, **two Scheduler entries at 14:00 and 15:00 UTC, decided 2026-09-23** — the job's own clock guard runs only the one that lands in 10:00–10:30 ET and skips market-closed days; Marcelo first set 13:30/14:30 UTC = 09:30 ET, which the guard would have skipped every day). Replaces the standalone 1:30 PM UTC `job:trade-alerts` entry (to be removed from Scheduler at the same time). Consequence accepted 2026-09-23: Trade Alerts now arrive ~10:20–10:30 ET instead of ~09:35; the Signals screen is live from 09:30 on the previous day's surface, so trading at the open is not blocked. Entries take effect once the Signals build is deployed (job + tables live in it); first run to be done manually with `--force` to check timing; add `option_chain_capture` + `option_surface_fit` to the watchdog's daily list. *Previous entry, now superseded:* **Trade Alert generation** (`job:trade-alerts`) — **confirmed 2026-09-05 against the actual Heroku Scheduler config: only one run/day, 1:30 PM UTC** (market open, added 2026-08-25 so a full freshly-scanned list exists right at open). The second, 10:00 PM UTC EOD run this doc previously described as also configured is not present in Scheduler — either never added or removed at some point; this doc's other references to a 22:00 UTC trade-alert run (and the "buffer before it" framing on the calendar-capture job below) were stale. Redesigned 2026-08-27 — see "Currently open" above.
4. **IBKR Gateway health check** (`ibkr_health_check`, every 10 minutes — changed from hourly 2026-08-29, confirmed via Scheduler dashboard screenshot 2026-09-08) — real handshake-based check (fixed 2026-08-21 after a `nc -z`-only version stayed green during a real outage), plus the worker-systemd check and the position-reconciliation drift check (see Order execution platform).
5. **Watchdog** (`job:watchdog`, daily 10:30 PM UTC) — checks that every daily job (market-data, P&L snapshot, trade alerts, calendar capture, screener scan) ran today (jobs 6-7 added to the check 2026-09-18), health-check ran within 30 min (was 2h; tightened 2026-09-18 since the check runs every 10 min), nothing stuck `running` 30+ min. Telegram-only on actual findings.
6. **Daily calendar capture** (`job:daily-calendar-capture`, 8:00 PM UTC, added 2026-08-31) — TradingView earnings/ex-dividend/economic-calendar data feeding the calendar-conflict trade-alert filter (see "Currently open" above); no IBKR/market-hours dependency. Runs after the day's only trade-alert generation (1:30 PM UTC), so that run always uses the previous day's calendar data — acceptable since these dates rarely change intraday. Manually triggered once to confirm clean in prod: 16/17 tickers resolved, 32 earnings rows, 5 dividend rows, 366 economic events.
7. **Daily screener scan** (`job:daily-screener-scan`, 6:00 PM UTC — moved from 11:00 PM 2026-09-09 because the scanner only ranks during regular trading hours; added 2026-09-05) — IBKR market-scanner discovery job feeding the Screener tab's `screener_scan_results` table (see "Currently open" above). Cleanly spaced after market-data/pnl-snapshot/watchdog.

### Telegram notification rules
- Fail-safe ordering: `job_runs` written first, Telegram attempted after, never masks a job failure.
- Alerts on every failure, all jobs. Trade Alerts notifies on every new batch. No daily success heartbeat otherwise.
- **Failure summaries + recovery notices (added 2026-09-12, `src/lib/runJob.ts`)** — prompted by a real ~3h20m `ibkr_health_check` outage (2026-09-12 04:50–08:10 UTC, IBKR's own login servers unreachable from the VPS overnight, self-resolved, not a code/infra bug) that produced 20 consecutive failure alerts, each dumping a ~3KB `docker logs` blob into Telegram, and then recovered completely silently. Two changes: (1) `telegramFailureSummary()` truncates the Telegram-bound failure text at the first `"): "` marker (kept full in `job_runs.error_message`) — every restart-script-backed failure message already puts its one-sentence diagnostic before that marker, so this reliably drops just the raw log dump; (2) `findPrecedingFailureStreak()` sends `✅ {job} recovered after N failed attempts (was down since <ts>, ~<duration>)` the first time a job succeeds after failing, closing the gap PROGRESS.md previously flagged as blocked on `job_runs` existing (it now does). New `src/lib/formatDurationHuman.ts` helper for the duration string. Both functions exported from `runJob.ts` for reuse by `tmp/simulateJobNotifications.ts`, a replay script that reconstructs what would have been sent for any historical window straight from `job_runs` — used to verify this change against the last 7 days before shipping (26 notifications reconstructed, including today's incident correctly summarized as 20 failed attempts / ~3h19m downtime).

## Staging environment (built 2026-09-21; design of record and open items below)

**Design decisions 2026-09-21 (Marcelo approved; supersede anything below that conflicts). Execution starting now, step 0 = read-only inventory.**
- **Cutover concept**: today's "current" apps hold paper (i.e. staging) data. New `iorio-staging-*` apps take a one-time full copy of current's DB and pick up exactly where current is; then current is frozen and stays dormant as the future prod (built in a later phase). Old FE shows a "prod is being built, use staging" maintenance page with a link to `staging.ioriore.com`. Freeze order: confirm no orders in flight, stop current worker, **delete/disable current's Scheduler entries** (Scheduler one-offs ignore `ps:scale web=0` and maintenance mode — the `ibkr_health_check` would keep restarting staging's Gateway; UI-only, Marcelo does it, screenshot first), disable GitHub auto-deploy on the old apps. **Real Scheduler list (dashboard screenshot 2026-09-21; 8 jobs, not 7; all Basic dynos, all UTC, `node dist/scripts/<file>.js`)**: `check-ibkr-health` every 10 min; `run-trade-alert-generation-job` 13:30; `run-daily-screener-scan-job` 18:00; `run-daily-calendar-capture-job` 20:00; `run-daily-market-data-job` 21:00; `run-daily-pnl-snapshot-job` 21:30; `run-daily-expiry-settlement-audit-job` 22:00; `run-watchdog-job` 22:30. Staging gets identical copies. Cutover window = after 22:30 UTC until 13:30 UTC (trade alerts at 13:30 = US open). Step-0 inventory findings: both apps in region eu, owned by Juan's Heroku account (new apps will be Juan-owned too, accepted); no pipelines, no `pg:backups` schedule on prod (gap), dyno metadata not enabled; VPS worker at 76d03a9 vs main ~10 commits ahead (drift exists today); `/opt/ibkr` (compose for paper AND live in one file, image `:latest` unpinned, restart-gateway.sh/check-worker.sh hardcode paper/`iorio-worker`) is not in git; second worker health-check key `IORIO_WORKER_HEALTHCHECK_SSH_PRIVATE_KEY_BASE64`; extra config vars `MARKETDATA_API_TOKEN`, `OPENROUTER_API_KEY`, `GENOSUKE_ENABLED`; the existing Cloudflare token is WAF-only.
- **Two pipelines** (API repo, FE repo), not one. `main` auto-deploys to staging only (with CI-gated deploy); prod is promotion only. FE gets runtime config (`/config.js` served by `server.js` from an env var) so the identical build is promoted, not rebuilt.
- **Full isolation staging vs prod**: separate DB, `SESSION_SECRET`, tunnel + health-check SSH keys (tunnel key `permitopen`-restricted to 4002 staging / 4001 prod), Better Stack sources, Flex token, Cloudflare (same zone: staging gets no write token; single-level hostnames for Universal SSL; ACM records DNS-only until issued). **No prod-to-staging DB copy ever** (different IBKR accounts). The cutover copy is the one exception (current is paper data). The in-app DB-sync screen is dropped; `sync-dev-db` is retargeted to pull **staging to laptop only** and refuses any other app.
- **Deploy = Option A, release phase does everything** (no laptop orchestrator): `migrate:latest:prod`, then an idempotent forced-command SSH deploy of the worker AND the versioned Gateway config (`/opt/ibkr` compose + scripts kept in the repo; restart the container only when the config hash changed) pinned to `HEROKU_SLUG_COMMIT` (needs `runtime-dyno-metadata`). Additive-only migrations stay mandatory. Backfills = knex migrations (quick) or tracked data jobs (long). Worker heartbeat stores its SHA; `job:watchdog` alerts on drift vs the latest Heroku release. Post-boot web self-check posts "release live" to Telegram. Daily `pg:backups` scheduled on both DBs (no Heroku API token in app config). Fail-closed if the VPS is unreachable, with an escape-hatch config var that skips the worker step (setting it triggers a release; the override is announced in Telegram). Live-Gateway auto-restart stays behind an explicit confirm flag until a live restart is proven without 2FA (live container has never connected).
- **Deploy guards**: order-flow is DAY-only everywhere (`TimeInForce.DAY` in both `buildOrder` paths, Adaptive algo), so any `order_requests` row in `confirmed`/`cancel_requested`/`partially_filled`/`submitted` while the market is closed is anomalous. Prod release aborts on any of them, or when the market is open (market calendar table, ~16:15 ET buffer). Staging warns in Telegram and proceeds (else every daytime push would fail). Both have the announced override. A guard-aborted release is not retried automatically. To build: SIGTERM handler in the worker (finish the in-progress order handler, then exit) and an empirical restart test on staging (mid-placement order), since no signal handler exists today.
- **Telegram**: verbose per-step deploy messages (start, guards, migrations, worker SHA old to new, Gateway config, release live/self-check, failing step). Trim later if noisy. Current ops channel + bot = prod (goes quiet until prod is built). Staging gets its own channel (Marcelo + a new Genosuke-staging bot). Two Genosuke bots, one per env. Alerts also prefixed `[STAGING]` on staging.
- **LIVE/PAPER + environment labelling**: top bar always shows PAPER or LIVE; a small STAGING tag only when the environment isn't production. The value comes from the worker's real IBKR account (`managedAccounts`; paper IDs start `DU`, to be verified), never guessed from config. **Fail closed** on mismatch with `IBKR_TRADING_MODE`, and the DB is bound to a specific IBKR account ID on first connect (mismatch = crossed DB/account). On mismatch: no orders, no IBKR-to-DB writes (no reconcile), throttled Telegram alert, red top-bar state, Pulse card. Enforced in the single IBKR connection factory (verify all connect paths use it). Re-binding is an explicit admin script, no override var. Needs a UI mockup Artifact approved before FE work.

Cloudflare free plan cannot serve a custom block/maintenance page (`not entitled to use a custom response`; also only 4xx allowed): maintenance page will be Heroku maintenance mode + `MAINTENANCE_PAGE_URL` hosted on the staging FE. **Gotchas**: (1) `heroku pg:copy --confirm` = target DB name; (2) local `manage-user.ts`/psql against Heroku needs `NODE_ENV=production` (SSL) — `PGSSLMODE=require` alone fails; (3) `checkWorkerOnVps` parses the literal text `iorio-worker.service` in check-worker.sh output, so a per-env script must keep that marker (staging script `/opt/ibkr/check-worker-staging.sh` prints it) — proper env-neutral marker is Phase B; (4) the Cloudflare setup-token file got overwritten by a stray `pbpaste` once; (5) `heroku ps:type web=basic` after the first deploy (new apps default Eco); (6) Eco pool showed 54% remaining at cutover. Temp user `claude-test` exists in the STAGING DB only, delete when testing is done.
