- **Genosuke cold-start 401: ROOT-CAUSED and fixed, 2026-09-22 (`2111fe6`, verified on staging)**: express-session sends every byte of a login response except the last immediately and holds that last byte until the session is saved to Postgres; Genosuke's client never read the login body on success, so its first request after a dyno restart raced the save (log proof: same cookie sid, but the store had no session yet). Fix: read the whole login body before using the cookie (regression test fails without it). After the fix a cold start logged in and made tool calls with no 401. Same session also verified: readable Yes/Cancel cards (close BE showed BUY BACK call / SELL shares, Cancel created no order row); `list_positions` returns all 7 open positions incl. any unstructured; temporary diagnostics reverted.
- **ITM expiry at the source, built 2026-09-22 (`9e77f2b`; worker deploy still manual)**: my original plan (classify from IBKR position changes) was WRONG: the audit's header documents that the reconcile loop cannot tell a call-away from IBKR's transient held-positions gap at settlement, so the objective daily-bar test stays the classifier. New: `correctExpirySettlementsNow` runs the audit's own apply step at the end of any reconciliation pass that closed a leg (not only expired options, because the stock leg can close a pass later), shrinking the wrong-record window from up to ~a day to about a minute; the nightly job stays as the safety net. Mode/summary code is shared (`readExpirySettlementMode`, `summarizeExpirySettlement`). **The worker now REQUIRES `EXPIRY_SETTLEMENT_MODE` in its `.env` at startup** (staging Heroku is `apply`); add it to `/opt/iorio-worker-staging/.env` BEFORE deploying the worker or it will crash-loop. The end-to-end path can only be seen at a real ITM expiry (staging TLT $81 and SPY $765 puts expire 2026-09-23). Still skipped for manual review: the AAOI $107 put (ITM at $106.23, assigned shares not tracked as a leg).
- **Other**: `scripts/deploy-worker-to-vps.sh` now needs `staging` or `live` (`live` also asks to type the word); `npm run deploy:worker:staging` added. `sync-dev-db` re-verified (row counts identical, no orphan failure) but it replaces the local schema, so other sessions' local-only tables must be re-migrated (`npm run migrate:latest`; done for `option_chain_snapshots` and `ticker_backfill_runs`, their local data is gone). Annualised return: parked by Marcelo (proposed formula: bucket total / sum(capital x years deployed), min 1 day per deployment). Genosuke's TLT 5x P81 order left partially filled (1 of 5) on purpose; single-leg orders are plain limit orders and can partially fill, only multi-leg combos are atomic across legs. All-or-none test on the paper account approved for market hours (unknown whether IBKR accepts AON with the Adaptive algo).
- **Full nightly job cycle PASSED on staging, 2026-09-21**: trade alerts 13:30, screener 18:00, calendar 20:00, market data 21:00, P&L snapshot 21:30, expiry audit 22:00 and watchdog 22:30 (`problems: []`) all `success`, plus the 10-minute health checks.
- **Worker-restart Telegram alert VERIFIED on staging, 2026-09-22**: the first check (2026-09-21 05:52 UTC stop) could not be confirmed after the fact, so it was repeated with `npm run worker:staging -- stop` at 22:36:22 UTC; the 22:40:45 health check restarted the worker (`restarted: true`, active again 22:40:47) and Marcelo confirmed the "iorio-worker.service was inactive — restarted successfully, now active." message in the staging group at 22:40 UTC (screenshot). New `scripts/staging-worker-control.sh` (`npm run worker:staging -- status|logs|stop|start|restart`, staging unit only, no live target) plus exact-match permission rules in `.claude/settings.local.json` made this possible without arbitrary SSH.
- **Staging worker on `9e77f2b` since 2026-09-21 22:17 UTC** (deployed by Marcelo with `npm run deploy:worker:staging`; `EXPIRY_SETTLEMENT_MODE=apply` added to its `.env`, `.env.bak-wp1` removed): `worker_health` shows sha 9e77f2b, staging, DUR854038, paper/paper; no startup errors.
- **Phase B WP2 (fail-closed IBKR account binding) BUILT and DEPLOYED to staging, 2026-09-22 (`6966b69`)**: design approved by Marcelo (env var per environment; on mismatch pause orders AND reconciliation, cancels stay allowed; a blocked order is marked `error` and never retried; API also refuses to confirm). Worker: `IBKR_EXPECTED_ACCOUNT_ID` (singular, exactly one id, required, in the VPS `.env` and validated at startup together with `IBKR_TRADING_MODE` and `APP_ENVIRONMENT`; a live account outside production is refused) is compared with the accounts the Gateway reports (`src/lib/accountBinding.ts`, `src/ibkr/ibkrGatewayAccountBinding.ts`); status ok/mismatch/pending is written to new `worker_health.account_binding_status/_reason` columns. Gates: `processOrderRequest` (mismatch -> order `error` + Telegram; pending, e.g. just reconnected -> left `confirmed` to retry), `reconcilePositionsFromIbkr` (skipped unless ok), `recordExecution` (ignored on mismatch), every order now carries `order.account`. The accounts list is cleared at each connect attempt so an old session's list can never make a new one look bound. State-based alerts via `alert_state` key `account_binding_mismatch` (down + recovery, 30 min reminders). API: `POST /positions/orders/:id/confirm` returns 409 unless `worker_health` is fresh (<=120 s), same `app_environment`, and binding ok (`src/lib/tradingGate.ts`; needs `APP_ENVIRONMENT` on the API, set on Heroku staging 2026-09-22). **Verified**: 319 tests, migration up/down on a scratch DB, and live on staging the worker reports binding `ok` ("Bound to DUR854038") ~10 s after startup. **Negative test PASSED on staging, 2026-09-22**: with the VPS `.env` set to a wrong `IBKR_EXPECTED_ACCOUNT_ID` (DUR000000) and the worker restarted, `worker_health` showed `mismatch` ("Expected account DUR000000, but the Gateway reports DUR854038") within ~10 s, reconciliation was skipped each minute, the `account_binding_mismatch` alert row and Telegram alert appeared, `POST /positions/orders/:id/confirm` returned 409 with that reason and left the order `pending_confirmation`, and a synthetic `confirmed` order inserted straight into the DB became `error` ("Trading blocked by account binding…") with no `ibkr_order_id` (never sent). Restoring DUR854038 and restarting returned binding to `ok`, resumed reconciliation and cleared the alert state (recovery message path). Test rows left in `order_requests`: one cancelled API-test order and one `error` synthetic order. **Still not tested**: that IBKR accepts the `order.account` stamp on a real paper order (one small paper order + cancel via the UI, Marcelo to OK). Rollout order that worked: `APP_ENVIRONMENT` on Heroku, `IBKR_EXPECTED_ACCOUNT_ID` in the VPS `.env`, push (migration), then `npm run deploy:worker:staging`; between API and worker deploys confirms were blocked, as designed. **Prod (later)**: needs `APP_ENVIRONMENT=production` on the prod API and `IBKR_EXPECTED_ACCOUNT_ID` in the prod worker `.env` BEFORE their code is deployed.
- **Phase B WP4 (atomic release-phase worker deploy) IN PROGRESS, 2026-09-22.** Marcelo rejected a post-hoc drift-detection watchdog check ("by the time the job runs, we already have wrong versions running — that's unacceptable") in favour of coupling the worker deploy into the API's Heroku release phase so both are provably on the same commit, always — matches the design already on record above ("Deploy = Option A, release phase does everything"). Built so far, both live on staging: (1) **worker SIGTERM handler** (commit `1b511ee`) stops starting new order/cancel handling and reconciliation passes on SIGTERM, drains a bounded 25s for whatever's already running (unit-tested via `waitUntilDrained`), then exits — anything not yet started is safely picked up by the next process's existing 30s poll fallback / initial-reconciliation-on-connect. Verified live on staging via two real restarts: clean "drained cleanly in 0ms" in the idle case (the realistic common case, given order placement is single-digit ms and reconciliation ~2s against a 60s gap between passes — manually racing a restart into a live in-flight window proved impractical by hand, so the actual wait/timeout logic is proven via fake-timer unit tests instead). (2) **worker code hash** (`computeSourceClosureHash.ts`, commit `d1dc7bd`): statically walks the worker entrypoint's transitive relative-import closure (not a hand-maintained file list) plus `package-lock.json`, worker publishes it to new `worker_health.worker_code_hash` at startup. Verified: computing it independently from a fresh local checkout at the same commit produced the byte-identical hash to what the VPS worker published (`c025a7fc...539fa8e`), proving the release phase (a third checkout) will compare correctly. Marcelo asked for messaging either way: every release-phase run will announce whether the worker was deployed or skipped (and why), not just on deploy. **Still to build**: atomic build-then-swap worker deploy script (the current `deploy-worker-to-vps.sh` does `git reset --hard` then builds in place — a failed build leaves the source tree changed under the still-running old process; needs build-to-a-fresh-location, verify, then swap+restart, keeping the last-known-good build on disk), wiring the hash-skip decision + guards + verbose Telegram messaging into the API's `release:` script, then prove the whole thing end to end on staging before touching prod's release phase.
- **Still to test on staging**: the all-or-none order test at market open, the first real ITM expiry through the new worker hook (TLT $81 / SPY $765 puts, 2026-09-23), and a redo of the FE rollback drill with two visibly different bundles. Then Phase B (deployment machinery) starts, beginning with the three WP1 approvals in item 5.

**Concept, clarified 2026-08-31 (supersedes the original framing below)**: this is not "rename current to staging, build a new prod." A brand-new staging environment is built with entirely new Heroku apps/domains/database, active immediately for ongoing paper-trading development. **Current stays as the future production identity** — untouched, kept running until staging is verified working, then **paused** (dynos scaled to 0, worker systemd unit stopped) and left dormant. Current is only woken again for the live cutover (flip `IBKR_TRADING_MODE` to `live`, start the already-existing-but-`Exited(1)` `ib-gateway-live` container, resume current's worker) — a separate future decision gated on Juan confirming the live IBKR connection actually works end-to-end (see the still-open real-time-market-data thread above). Building staging carries zero live-money risk.

**Research finding that simplifies this a lot**: the VPS already has a second IBKR Gateway container defined (`ib-gateway-live`, port 4001, its own `.env.live`, currently stopped) and `IBKR_TRADING_MODE` is already a one-var paper/live switch on both the web app and worker — so the live side of this needs no new IBKR container, just getting that existing one to actually connect (separate blocked item) and un-pausing current.

**Correction, 2026-08-31**: Marcelo initially recalled "we already have a second worker deployed, one for paper and one for live" — checked directly via SSH and this isn't accurate today. There is exactly **one** systemd unit (`iorio-worker.service`) and **one** `/opt/iorio-worker` clone on the VPS, currently pointed at paper (`IBKR_TRADING_MODE=paper` in its `.env`). What actually exists as a pair is the two **Gateway containers** (`iorio-ibkr-ib-gateway-paper-1`, running; `iorio-ibkr-ib-gateway-live-1`, `Exited (1)`) — not two workers. Likely the source of the mix-up. Naming is designed below to prevent this recurring.

**Decided design**:
- **New apps**: `iorio-staging-api` / `iorio-staging-app`, domains `api-staging.ioriore.com` / `staging.ioriore.com`. Own Postgres addon (mini/essential-1). Own Scheduler addon too — reversed 2026-08-31 from the original "no scheduled jobs" requirement: Marcelo wants staging to replicate prod as closely as possible, so it gets its own copy of all 7 scheduled-job entries (`job:daily-market-data`, `job:daily-pnl-snapshot`, `job:trade-alerts` (once/day), `ibkr_health_check`, `job:watchdog`, `job:daily-calendar-capture`, `job:daily-screener-scan`), pointed at staging's own app/DB, running independently of current's.
- **Heroku Pipeline**, both apps: staging stage = the new `iorio-staging-*` apps (today's GitHub auto-deploy from `main` moves onto these). Production stage = today's existing `iorio-reloaded-*` apps (dormant now; woken and promoted-into only for the live cutover). Chosen over two fully independent apps because slug promotion guarantees the exact tested build reaches "prod" rather than a second independent rebuild from source — real benefit on the API side; on the frontend it's mostly cosmetic since `VITE_API_BASE_URL` is baked in at build time regardless (see `src/api/client.ts`), so the frontend still needs its own per-environment build either way.
- **VPS worker naming, to prevent the confusion above from recurring**: current's worker gets renamed `iorio-worker-prod` (`/opt/iorio-worker-prod`, systemd unit `iorio-worker-prod.service`) — still pointed at paper today, will point at live after cutover, but the name reflects its permanent *role* (prod), not its current trading mode. Staging gets a new, separately-named `iorio-worker-staging` (`/opt/iorio-worker-staging`, systemd unit `iorio-worker-staging.service`). Both are just directory/unit renames plus a fresh clone for staging — no code changes. Own DB connection, own distinct persistent-connection clientId for staging (not the existing fixed `42`, to avoid any ambiguity even though they'll never run concurrently — see below).
- **Shared paper Gateway container, by design, never concurrently**: staging's worker connects to the *same* paper Gateway (port 4002, same underlying IBKR paper account) current uses today — confirmed acceptable specifically because current gets paused before staging's worker ever starts. Running both workers against the one shared paper account at the same time was explicitly rejected 2026-08-31 ("we can't have two services pointing to the same gateway, it'll be chaotic and it's not a true test of how the app should work") — reconciliation on each side would ingest the other's trades as unexplained position changes. The cutover is a deliberate manual switch: stop `iorio-worker-prod`, then start `iorio-worker-staging`.
- **DB sync**: a real in-app screen (not just a script) to pull current's Postgres into staging's, mirroring [[project_sync_dev_db_script]]'s mechanics. Built as part of this pass, not deferred.
- **Genosuke/Telegram**: staging needs its own BotFather bot token (Telegram allows only one webhook URL per bot token, so it can't share current's) — a manual step only Marcelo can do — plus its own chat/channel and `GENOSUKE_*` config vars on `iorio-staging-api`.
- **Rollback**: Heroku's built-in release rollback covers app code for free once the apps exist. DB migrations aren't automatically reversible — the real strategy is the additive-only-migrations discipline already in practice ([[project_pre_launch_migrations_editable]], superseded note), not new down-migration tooling.

**Housekeeping fixes, thought through 2026-08-31, bundled in regardless of staging** (Marcelo asked for the analysis, not just the flag):
- [x] **`Procfile`'s dead `worker:` line — fixed 2026-09-05.** Deleted entirely (was `worker: node dist/src/ibkrWorker.js`, pointing at a file deleted long ago). The worker's real, permanent home is the VPS by deliberate design ([[project_worker_runs_on_vps]]) — a dead line was a real risk if ever scaled up by mistake against the shared IBKR Gateway. Still needs the same fix applied to the staging Procfile whenever that's built (same file, both apps).
- [x] **`SESSION_SECRET` silent fallback — fixed 2026-09-05.** `src/config/env.ts` now requires it via `requireEnvironmentVariable`, throwing at boot instead of silently accepting a hardcoded dev-only string. Confirmed set on Heroku prod (`iorio-reloaded-api`) before deploying this; added a real generated value to local `.env` (gitignored) so local dev keeps working. Typecheck + full test suite (36/36) confirmed clean.
- **`.env.live.example` key-name mismatch** — still open, lives on the VPS not this repo. Documents `TWS_USERID_LIVE`/`TWS_PASSWORD_LIVE`/`AUTO_RESTART_TIME_LIVE`, but the real `/opt/ibkr/.env.live` uses the unsuffixed names the compose file actually interpolates (`TWS_USERID`, `TWS_PASSWORD`, `AUTO_RESTART_TIME`). Low priority (documentation only) — fix directly over SSH whenever convenient.

**Deferred to when the live cutover actually happens, not part of this build**: a `restart-gateway-live.sh` + forced-command SSH key for the live container's health-check (today's `/opt/ibkr/restart-gateway.sh` is hardcoded to the paper container name); deciding whether current's DB carries forward its paper-era history into real prod or starts clean; a live-specific smoke test.

## Ticker Detail on a shared live connection (2026-09-19)
`streamTickerDetail` now borrows `sharedLiveConnection` (one-shot fallback kept): a second shared connection whose market data type is set to REALTIME once at connect and never changed (`requestRealtimeMarketData` is a no-op for it, via `markMarketDataTypeManaged`). All reqIds on shared connections come from that connection's counter through `nextReqIdFor(ib, fallback)` (fetchOptionChain lookups/quotes, priceBarCache's derived IV-bar id which used `reqId + 1000`, and the four fixed ids in streamTickerDetail). Fixed `ib.once()` misuse in `lookupPricingSnapshot` (`tickSnapshotEnd`) and `lookupContractDetails` (`contractDetailsEnd`); `lookupPricingSnapshot` now also cleans up its listeners and cancels its subscription on timeout.
- **Tested (Saturday, market closed):** solo vs 3 tickers concurrent (AAOI/NBIS/SPCX): identical prices, contract sets, chart bars, technicals; the one quote difference was a genuine late tick on the correct contract (SPCX 2026-09-25 152.5C, delta matches that position's leg). Listener counts on the live connection return to the idle baseline within 20s after full sessions and after disconnects at 2s/4s/7s. One-shot fallback verified by requesting before the live connection was ready. First data ~0.6s once warm (the connect used to come first); option chain ~10s (unchanged work: strike checks + quotes).
- **Known limitation:** if the shared live connection drops mid-session, open modals stop updating until reopened (same as a dropped one-shot connection); not handled.
- **Not migrated (same reqId refactor still needed):** `streamPositionQuote`, `streamOrderLegQuote`, `fetchTickerQuoteSnapshot`, `fetchTickerOverview`, `refreshTradeAlert`, `evaluateRollForPosition`, and others; they still use fixed ids on one-shot connections, which is safe as-is.

## Dashboard Portfolio-card load time (2026-09-19)
Measured, not guessed: the exposure SSE stream took ~9s to first data (account+CSP query ~1.0s, DB 4ms, new SSH tunnel ~3.7s + IBKR handshake ~1.7s per stream, FROZEN price ticks ~2.1s). Not a market-hours effect. Fixes: `streamLivePrices` and `streamLiveGreeks` now borrow the shared read connection (one-shot fallback kept); the exposure route starts the account fetch in parallel with the stream; `server.ts` warms the shared connection at startup. Result: Portfolio card ~2.6s in the browser (desktop + mobile checked), first request after a restart ~2.3s, greeks stream first data ~1.6s.
- **Not migrated yet (needs a reqId refactor first):** `streamTickerDetail`, `streamPositionQuote`, `streamOrderLegQuote`, `fetchTickerQuoteSnapshot`, `fetchTickerOverview`, option-chain helpers, and ~10 more. They use fixed/module-level reqIds (1, 2, 5,000+, 10,000+) that only avoid collisions on a private socket; on the shared connection concurrent users would cross-resolve each other's prices. Background jobs (daily closing prices, ledger P&L, alert generation) are lower value. `checkIbkrHealthJob` must stay one-shot.
- **clientIds (changed later 2026-09-19):** the shared connections no longer use a fixed id. Each connect attempt draws a random id from its own range (read 1,000,000-1,499,999; live 1,500,000-1,999,999), above the one-shot connections (0-999,999) and the worker's fixed 42. A fixed 44 had let two processes booting together (duplicate dev servers) hang each other for 18.7s.

## Outage alert throttling (2026-09-19, built and deployed, verified in a real outage)
IBKR maintenance on a Saturday produced a Telegram message per systemd restart (worker) and per 10-min run (health check). Fix: state-based alerting. New `alert_state` table (row present = already alerted) and `src/lib/throttledAlert.ts` (`notifyDownThrottled`, `clearDownState`; atomic, so concurrent callers can't double-send). (A) `runJob` takes `failureAlertReminderIntervalMs`; `ibkr_health_check` uses 1h: first failure alerts, same-message repeats are muted, a changed message alerts immediately, recovery uses the existing streak message. (B) `persistentIbkrConnection.start()` no longer rejects on a failed connect; the worker stays up and retries with the existing backoff, so systemd stops restart-looping. The worker alerts once after 5 min disconnected (was 10; lowered 2026-09-19), reminds hourly, and sends one recovery message. Tested locally: throttle sequence (10 cases incl. concurrency), `start()` resolving with an unreachable tunnel, `runJob` 3 failures = 1 alert + recovery + state cleared. **Not tested:** a real Gateway outage end to end. **Deploy order:** GitHub push first (release phase creates `alert_state`), then `npm run deploy:worker` (manual, VPS). Other worker startup failures (DB down, etc.) still crash-and-alert per restart. **Bug found live and fixed (8e378b1; reached the VPS at 17:08 UTC. A 16:53 redeploy shipped nothing new because `deploy:worker` deploys origin/main and the fix wasn't pushed yet, so push first and verify the VPS file):** the worker's disconnect message embedded the elapsed time, and the throttle treats a changed message as a new alert, so it re-alerted every minute. Throttled messages must be constant text; reminders add the duration themselves. The redeploy causes one extra alert (stored text differs), then hourly. **Verified live 2026-09-19 (real IBKR outage, Gateway back at 17:24 UTC):** worker sent exactly 1 alert (17:19), no repeats, state cleared on reconnect; health check sent 1 alert at 16:41 and stayed quiet. 5-min threshold deployed 17:29 UTC (VPS at 2e0ffad, dist file checked). **Deploy lesson:** `deploy:worker` ships origin/main, not the local tree; push first, read the script's stderr warning, and grep the deployed dist on the VPS before calling it done.

## HIGH PRIORITY: Monday 2026-09-21, market hours: retest shared-connection streams
The 2026-09-19 stream migration (`streamLivePrices`, `streamLiveGreeks` on the shared read connection, clientId 44) was only tested on a Saturday, when live ticks are thin. `reqMarketDataType` is connection-wide and `priceBarCache`, `fetchLivePrices` and both migrated streams already flip it between FROZEN/REALTIME on that one connection; `fetchTickerOverview.ts` records that a second call while a subscription was open once silently prevented its first tick. **Verify with the market open:** (1) two Dashboard tabs at once, both Portfolio cards get live updates after the first reading; (2) a Positions P&L fetch during an open exposure stream doesn't stall either; (3) greeks stream keeps updating. Any stall -> revert those two streams to one-shot. **Also verify the Ticker Detail modal on the live connection:** open 2-3 modals at once with the market open; bid/ask/last and option quotes keep ticking in each, values are right per ticker, and closing one does not stop the others. Any stall -> revert `streamTickerDetail` to `connectToIbkrGateway()`.

## Stream multiplexing (built)
The design plan (D1–D5) that used to be here was implemented: git commit `4341611` "Stream multiplexer (always on), Price Performance redesign, strategy badge standardisation". The original plan, findings and decision log are in `PROGRESS-ARCHIVE.md`.

## Optional / future ideas (not being built unless requested)
- **Live-stream connection cap in dev (low priority, parked 2026-09-19).** *Problem:* the local dev API is plain `http://localhost:3030` (HTTP/1.1), so Chrome allows only 6 connections to it across ALL tabs and every open SSE stream holds one. After the notification-stream sharing (`ca35031`) a Positions tab holds 3 (notifications, greeks, P&L) and an open Ticker Detail modal adds 3 (detail, greeks, P&L), so two tabs (one with a modal) can still starve every later stream. Symptom: P&L/price cells spin forever or requests time out while the servers are healthy — check open stream count before suspecting the backend. *Prod is NOT affected:* the deployed front end uses `https://api.ioriore.com` (verified via `heroku config:get VITE_API_BASE_URL -a iorio-reloaded-app`), which is Cloudflare-proxied to the Heroku router and negotiates HTTP/2, where MDN says concurrent streams are negotiated (default 100) instead of capped at 6. Not verified: several live tabs in prod (needs a prod login), Cloudflare's exact stream limit. The bare `iorio-reloaded-api-caecaa4e5c9e.herokuapp.com` address is HTTP/1.1 but nobody uses it (Heroku lists no custom domain because Cloudflare proxies to it). *Options considered:* (a) **HTTPS/HTTP/2 local proxy** (e.g. Caddy + mkcert in front of :3030, `VITE_API_BASE_URL` pointed at the https address) — ~30-60 min, zero app code, makes dev match prod's transport; needs Chrome to trust the cert and Playwright `ignoreHTTPSErrors`; do NOT try to make Express itself speak h2 (unreliable). Recommended if the pain recurs. (b) **Modal reuses the page's greeks/P&L streams** — −2 streams per tab, small change. (c) **Multiplex every per-tab stream onto one SSE** (Marcelo's preferred design — full design plan now in "Stream multiplexing — design plan" above; a legitimate, standard pattern, but no source documents it as the fix for this cap) — real costs: the request today lives in the stream URL (e.g. `?positionIds=a,b,c`), so a shared stream needs an explicit subscribe/unsubscribe call plus server-side per-connection subscription memory; on every reconnect the browser must re-send its subscriptions (the server frees that state on disconnect by design, to release IBKR market-data lines, which are capped); per-subscription cleanup or IBKR lines leak; Ticker Detail's must-include strikes (computed once at SSE open) and the synchronous SSE `onUpdate` contract need redesign; 11 EventSource call sites (Positions, Pulse, Dashboard, Trade Alerts, Price Performance, exposure, order quotes, notifications, ticker detail) = large regression surface to retest on desktop + mobile; estimate days not hours (low confidence). Head-of-line delay from one big message (option chain) is negligible at these payload sizes, and the server has no connection limit — the cap is browser-side. Safe to build with explicit subscribe/unsubscribe, resubscribe-on-reconnect, per-subscription cleanup and a server timeout that releases IBKR lines for dead clients. Only worth it for reasons beyond dev (e.g. fewer IBKR subscriptions per user); write the design for approval first. (d) **Cross-tab sharing** (SharedWorker, or BroadcastChannel + Web Locks leader election) — the standard fix for the multi-tab cap; reduces per-browser connections but not streams per tab. *Workaround now:* close extra Iorio tabs, hard-reload. *References:* MDN "Using server-sent events"; server-sent-events.com "Sharing One SSE Connection Across Tabs"; DEV Community "Understanding SSE and Why HTTP/2 Matters".
- Full historical backtesting engine using purchased historical options data (10 years) — cost/ToS permitting. Lightweight forward-simulation (self-accumulated data only) is planned instead, not optional.
- IBKR chart embedding for open positions — superseded by the decision to build our own via lightweight-charts.
- **Roll-vs-hold expected-value comparison (low priority, deferred 2026-08-31).** The net-credit floor above only proves a roll doesn't lose money on execution — it can't prove rolling beats *not* rolling over the rest of the position's life, since that depends on future stock price/IV paths nobody can know. A future version could model that as an explicit expected value (extending `blackScholesPop.ts`'s machinery to compare "roll now" vs. "hold to expiry/assignment"), but it would be a modeled estimate with real model risk (Black-Scholes assumptions, IV surface accuracy), not a guarantee — would need to be presented and labeled as a probabilistic signal, not a fact, if ever built.

- **Strategy badge standardisation (2026-09-20)**: one shared `StrategyBadge` (app `components/StrategyBadge.tsx`, palette in `lib/positionPnl.ts`): CC blue, CSP purple, N/S orange ("Unstructured" badge renamed N/S). Used by Positions, PositionCard, Ticker modal alerts, Trade Alerts, Trade Blotter, Cycle bucket badge, Dashboard events; Dashboard history chart and Pulse badges/allocation bar use the same Tabler colours. Spelled-out "Unstructured" text (tiles, tables, legends) not yet renamed — awaiting decision.
